# Receipt S1: Item 1, the issue-stamp checker

**Crew** S1-impl. **Date** 2026-09-18. **Tree** `fluid-editing` at `4b9565ad` plus the working-tree
diff below (not committed; the driver holds git). Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1/`.

Tags: **MEASURED** (run, output quoted), **READ** (from source), **INFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**.

## Summary

The suite now passes, on both arms, in every stranger shape measured: a full clone into `xschem`,
a `--depth 1` clone, a `git archive` export with no `.git`, a normal clone named `xschem-claude`,
a git worktree named `wt`, an export unpacked *inside another repository*, and the main tree.
In a full clone nothing is skipped (60 checks). In a shallow clone 2 rows skip by name
(58 + 2). In an export 5 rows skip by name (55 + 5). Nine sabotages each turned the rows meant to
catch them red. In the no-`.git` export, T1 went from `counted_failures=33` to `29`, and the only
per-case change is `test_issue_stamp 4 -> 0`. The 14 cases still red are other suites (DECISIONS
D1), listed in section 7. Nine of them read their corpus through git; the other five are not git
failures at all.

**Two things the driver needs before the gate** (section 9): the persistent `:99` on this box is
**not the user's dev display**. It is the outsider audit's leftover Xvfb (F36, live), so a T1 from
the real HOME will almost certainly score all 11 display cases NODISPLAY. Also, T1's `results.log`
cannot show that a case skipped rows.

## 3. Why each red happens (step 2)

* **S20, folder name.** READ: the row expected `{1 xschem-claude}` from `puts [file tail
  $istamp::repo]`. The property it was written for (BC1: `init` must not derive the repo from
  `[info script]` at call time) is about *which directory*, not its name. In every measured
  failure `rev_exists` was 1, so the checker had found the right repository. A clone called
  `xschem-claude` passed by coincidence.
* **Rows that need a repository with HEAD, but not history** (present in a shallow clone, absent
  from an export). READ: `istamp_test_rev` (`git rev-parse --short=N HEAD`) supplies `$REV`, and
  S0's `rev_exists $REV`, S20's driver, and Q1/Q2's `git show $REV:tests/banner_rule.tcl` all need
  it. G2, B3, A2-A4 and N1-N2 also run the gate on `tree=$REV`. With `$REV` empty, parsing fails
  first: that is S0, S15 and S15c. The gate rows would also have failed if the suite had reached
  them.
* **Rows that need history older than HEAD.** D9, because every real stamp names `61af3692`.
  MEASURED: all 10 are ancestors of HEAD (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`), so a
  `--single-branch` full clone still resolves them (INFERRED). G4 also belongs here, as a matter
  of semantics: in a shallow clone, "`deadbee` does not resolve" cannot be told apart from
  "`deadbee` lies beyond the depth".
* **No git repository at all.** `git rev-parse` fails, so `$REV` is empty. The driver script
  was built by string interpolation, so it contained `puts [istamp::rev_exists ]`. Its syntax
  error escaped through the unguarded `exec` inside `check`'s arguments and killed the suite at
  S20. The rows after S20 never ran, the cleanup at the end never ran (hence the `drv_<pid>`
  corpse), and the xschem arm exited 0. Inside another repository, git does not fail: it
  answers with **that** repository's HEAD and history (shape d).
* **Rows that never needed git.** MEASURED as unaffected: S1-S14, S16, S18, S19, B1, B2, B4,
  G1, G5, A1, A5, D9b and W1-W7. B1, B4 and D9's grammar/baseline/assert work are file and grep
  work.

## 4. The fix and the skip design (step 3)

**The checker** (`issue_stamp.tcl`, `proc istamp::history_probe`) classifies a tree once, at
`init`:

| state | evidence | what the gate does with an unresolved revision |
|---|---|---|
| `full` | `.git` here, `--show-prefix` empty, `--is-shallow-repository` = `false` | **problem (red)**, as before |
| `shallow` | same, but `--is-shallow-repository` = `true` | revisions that resolve are verified; those that do not are **NOT VERIFIED**, named in `last_skips` |
| `none` | no `.git` at this tree's root | git is **never asked** (`rev_verdict` returns `skip` before any exec), so a repository above cannot answer |
| `unreadable` | a `.git` git cannot read, git missing or timed out, or `--show-prefix` non-empty (another repo) | **treated as `full`: nothing skips, every revision question goes red** |

Quoted from the code, `proc istamp::rev_verdict`:
```
    if {$st eq "none"} { return skip }
    …
    if {[dict get $rev_cache $rev]} { return ok }
    if {$st eq "shallow"} { return skip }
    return bad
```
Only positive evidence of absence skips. Anything the probe cannot establish lands in `unreadable`,
which skips nothing (principle ii: never a silent pass). On `skip`, the gate **carries on**:
header window, grammar, baseline, `assert=` and the mirror check still run. Only the revision
question and `quote=` (which needs `git show <rev>:path`) are set aside. `gate` still returns only
problems, so no caller changed; `last_skips`/`last_verified` are new namespace variables. The CLI
prints `history: <state> -- <why>`, every `ISSUE-STAMP: NOT VERIFIED <item>`, and
`ISSUE-STAMP: ok (0 problems; 10 revision(s) NOT VERIFIED -- shallow: history absent)`.

**The suite** declares each row's need once:
```
set NEEDS_REPO    {S0 Q1 Q2}
set NEEDS_HISTORY {G4 D9h}
set NEEDS_GITBIN  {H1 H2 H3 H4 H5}
```
`check_needs` evaluates a row's script **only** if its need is met. Otherwise it prints
`skip: <row> -- NOT VERIFIED: <why>` and records the row in `::SKIPPED`. A skip never prints
`SKIP:` or `RESULT: SKIP` at column 0 (full_audit's and run_suites' whole-suite self-skip banners),
and a skip line never ends in `FAIL`. With no repository, `$REV` becomes the fixed legal token
`d64686a1` and git is not asked for HEAD, so the parse-level rows (S15, S15b, S15c, S17, G5, N3)
and the gate rows (B3, G2, A2-A4, N1-N2) still run.

**Principle by principle:**

* **(i) S20.** Compares the driver-side `$istamp::repo` with `$ROOT = [file dirname ×3 [file normalize
  [info script]]]`, derived from the *suite's* own location, so no folder name appears anywhere.
  +S20b: the driver's checker must reach the same history verdict and, where a repo exists,
  resolve HEAD. The driver is now written with `[list]`, and its `exec` is caught: a dying
  driver is a named FAIL, never a suite death.
* **(ii) Named skips.** Shallow skips G4 and D9h. Export skips S0, G4, Q1, Q2 and D9h. Every
  other row runs.
* **(iii) Full clone.** Nothing skipped (H7). Red rows stay red (section 6). D9b's both-direction
  self-test passes.
* **(iv) No new vacuous green.** Seven new rows:
  - H1: the probe classifies four known fixture repositories (full, shallow, none, unreadable),
    and git-free disk evidence agrees.
  - H2-H5: the gate's verdict `{problems skipped verified}` on six known corpora in each state.
    H2 shows a full history **refuses** a bogus revision and a rotted quote. H4's `none` fixture
    sits *inside* the full fixture, so `{0 1 0}` proves git was not asked. H5 shows an
    unreadable `.git` skips nothing.
  - H6: this tree's verdict equals the disk evidence (`.git`, git's own `shallow` file, a
    worktree's `commondir`), read without git.
  - H7: the skipped set is **exactly** the declared set for the state, which in a full clone is
    empty.
  - D9h: every well-formed stamped revision in the real corpus was verified (at least one, none
    skipped).

  The H rows need only the git program, so they run in the shallow clone and the export too.
  They are what proves G4's refusal there.
* **(v) Banner.** A skipping run ends `RESULT: ALL PASS (58 checks, 2 skipped -- shallow:
  history absent)` / `OVERALL: ok` / exit 0. This is the trailer shape `test_ase_bus_bits_0159`
  already ships.

**Cost.** MEASURED: `exec timeout 30 true` costs 104 ms against 0.8 ms bare, and the file's rule
wraps every exec in `timeout`. A first version took 16.7 s. Probing once per fixture state,
building the fixtures in one `sh` exec and memoising revisions within one `gate()` brought it to
**7.0 s** (the original suite: 3.5 s). The net cost is about 3.5 s on a T1 of about 260-380 s.

## 6. It still goes RED on genuine defects: MEASURED

**Existing red rows** G1, G4, G5, Q2, A2, A4, B2 and B3 pass in the full clones (each asserts that
the checker refused its fixture), and D9b (selftest: 5 known-good, 10 known-bad, 2 round-trips)
passes.

**CLI on known-bad corpora** (`gate <issuesdir> <baseline>`):

| fixture | main / `xschem` (full) | shallow | export |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tree=deadbee` | **rc 1** `does not resolve` | rc 0 `NOT VERIFIED 9999: tree=deadbee` | rc 0 `NOT VERIFIED` |
| `fix=superseded`, no `super=` | **rc 1** `malformed stamp` | **rc 1** | **rc 1** |

**The honest limit, stated plainly.** Where history is absent, a bogus `tree=` cannot be told
apart from one beyond the depth, so it is named NOT VERIFIED, not refused. H2 proves the refusal
on a full fixture in every shape.

**Sabotages.** Each was applied to one literal (checked found exactly once) in a copy in the
shape, run on the tclsh arm, and restored; md5 was re-verified afterwards. Run twice, the second
time on the final files, with identical results.

| id | shape | sabotage | red rows (everything else green) |
|---|---|---|---|
| SB1 | full `xschem` | BC1's bug: `init` derives repo from `[info script]` at call time | **S20** (`got: …/xschem/tests/headless` / `want: …/xschem`), **S20b** (`got: none -` / `want: full 1`) |
| SB2 | full | probe misreads full as `shallow` | **H1, H2, H6** |
| SB3 | full | probe misreads full as `none` | **H1, H2, H6** |
| SB4 | full | unresolved revision always `skip` | **G4, H2, H5** |
| SB5 | full | `none` asks git anyway | **H4** |
| SB10 | full | `init <dir>` ignores its argument (the old bug) | **H1, H2, H3, H4** |
| SB6 | shallow | a skip silently counted as a pass | **H7** (`got:` empty / `want: D9h G4`) |
| SB7 | shallow | S0/Q1/Q2 start skipping in shallow, undeclared | **H7** (`got: D9h G4 Q1 Q2 S0`) |
| SB8 | shallow | shallow treated as full (the pre-fix behaviour) | **D9** (the original F24 red), **H3** |

In SB2 and SB3, H7 stays green by design: a misread state feeds both sides of H7. H6 is the row
that catches a misread, and it did.

**One audit claim does not reproduce here.** An F21 verifier wrote that variant_1470 (OT1) and
sp_1452 (SE1) were already red in a fresh-HOME git clone. MEASURED on this box: both pass in the
full-clone control (76 and 58 checks). The difference may be environmental (UNKNOWN).

**Per D1 none of the nine git suites was touched.** They do not share the checker's root cause
in code: each reads its corpus through `git ls-files`/`git rev-parse` and uses the error text as
data. They do share the remedy's shape, though: classify once, and skip a corpus-dependent row by
name where there is no `.git`. That is what a follow-up would reuse.

## 8. Constraints honoured

- **Manifest.** `md5sum -c --quiet …/xschem_manifest_fixes.md5` gave rc 0 before work, at an
  interim check, and after all work.
- **Scratch HOME.** Every xschem, tclsh and T1 run used a scratch HOME under
  `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1/`, with DISPLAY unset.
- **Nothing touched.** No devdisplay start/stop/view was run. `~/.claude/xschem_dev_display` was
  only read. Nothing was written in `~/dev/xschem-op-wcard`. No commits were made. No T1 was run
  in the main working tree.
- **git writes outside scratch: none.** `git worktree add` ran against the scratch clone only,
  never against the user's repository.

## S1-fix

**Crew** S1-fix. **Date** 2026-09-18. **Tree** `fluid-editing` at `bc61cd05`, with the two files
edited in place in the main tree (not committed; the driver holds git). Scratch:
`/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix/` (shapes in `shapes/`, logs in `logs/`, sabotages in `sab/`).
This section acts on the problems in `receipts/S1_verify.md`. Tags: **MEASURED**, **READ**,
**INFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**.

**Final md5:** `issue_stamp.tcl ab4e64bae975bab7bce80bcf2e197514`,
`test_issue_stamp.tcl 8069a362990b6aa7f121d119530ffc0e`. S1's bytes (`cd9b06b6…`/`618b0bab…`)
are kept in `s1fix/pre/`, and every RED-before row below ran on them.

### What changed

| # | problem | change |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The fixture builder inherited the caller's git environment (MUST) | `with_fixture_env` (suite) wraps the build **and** the checker's reads of the fixtures. It unsets the repo-locating variables, `GIT_CONFIG{,_PARAMETERS,_COUNT}`, `GIT_{AUTHOR,COMMITTER}_*`, `GIT_DEFAULT_HASH`, `GIT_DEFAULT_REF_FORMAT`, `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR` and `GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER`, and sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`. Every value is restored afterwards. `istamp_hist_fixtures` holds the wrapper itself. Its `sh` also runs `unset $(git rev-parse --local-env-vars)`, and `g()` gains `-c protocol.file.allow=always`. **New row H0** runs before any fixture is built: git's own local-env list must be covered, sentinels planted in every listed variable must be gone inside the wrapper and back after it, and system/global config must be off inside. |
| 2 | `unreadable` died at Q1 and left 7 corpses | `check_needs` catches a script that dies and scores it `ROW DIED: <first line>`. Q1 catches its own `git show` (`GIT SHOW FAILED: …`). When git cannot read HEAD in a tree with a `.git`, `$REV` falls back to the fixed token, and S0 (which now reads `$REV_HEAD`) is the single row that names the cause. Before this, S15, S15c and B3 went red on "tree= is not a revision", which named the wrong cause. The probe says "git is not installed (not on PATH)" when `auto_execok git` is empty, instead of the `timeout` program's `No such file or directory`. |
| 3 | `history_probe`'s code and its header disagreed about GIT_DIR | **Decided: GIT_DIR gets no vote.** `none` means no `.git` here, and git is never asked. Every checker git call goes through the new `istamp::git_in`, which removes `git_scrub` for the length of the exec. `git_scrub` is git's `--local-env-vars` list minus its three config variables, plus `GIT_NAMESPACE`. Config is **kept**, so D7's command-scope `safe.directory` still reaches the checker. The why is recorded above `history_probe`. **New row H10** plants `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE` pointing at the full fixture: `none` must stay `none`, and the foreign fixture must answer from its own history. |
| 4 | A re-initialised download probed `full`, and D9/D9h went red | **New state `foreign`.** The tree is not shallow, and `git rev-parse -q --verify <anchor>^{commit}` exits **1**, git's quiet "no such object". Any other failure (MEASURED: a corrupt object gives 128) is `unreadable`. The anchor is `7fe79fb2bf4230e3e1c52432082ccba804e868fc`, StefanSchippers' upstream "Initial commit" of 2020-08-08. It was chosen by measurement: `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` answers only this commit, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` puts it under all 23 local and remote refs. `rev_verdict` treats `foreign` like `shallow`: what resolves is verified, and what does not is NOT VERIFIED, by name. It is not treated like `none`, see deviations. Suite: `foreign` skips NEEDS_HISTORY (G4, D9h). New fixtures `foreign` and `corrupt`, and corpus G (`tree=f1`) in every hrow. **New rows H8** (the foreign fixture's verdict) and **H9** (this tree's full/foreign verdict against the real corpus: every stamped revision resolves in `full`, none does in `foreign`; NEEDS_DEPTH, so it skips in shallow and export). H6 maps `foreign` to its on-disk family, `full`. H7's declared sets include foreign and NEEDS_DEPTH. |
| 5 | Cosmetic: the trailer's skip reason when git is missing | The trailer names every cause, with one `## why:` line per cause. The CLI tail reads `foreign: this project's history absent`. |

Row count 60 → **64** (H0, H8, H9, H10). H1 now probes 6 fixtures, and every hrow has 7 corpora.
Probe cost: `--show-prefix` and `--is-shallow-repository` now run as **one** exec, so a full
tree probes in 2 execs (the anchor question is the second), the same count as before.

### Deviations

1. **Foreign asks git, the way shallow does, not the way `none` does.** The task said "skip
   loudly by name, like 'none'".
   - Every revision `foreign` cannot resolve is skipped by name, as asked.
   - What does resolve in the foreign history is verified. The repository is readable and HEAD
     is present, so S0, Q1 and Q2 keep running in a re-initialised download.
   - H8's G column pins this choice, and F4 shows the `none`-like variant reddening it.
2. **Item 3 reaches beyond the fixtures.** Every checker git call (real tree included) runs
   through `git_in`, with the repo-locating variables removed. Config variables are kept.
   - This is the chosen GIT_DIR behaviour. A consequence: a tree whose only link to its history
     is GIT_DIR (a bare repository plus `--work-tree`) is `none` and skips by name.
3. **The fixture env removes more than the minimum list.** It also removes:
   - author/committer, hash, ref-format and template variables;
   - `GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER`, git's simulation of a foreign-owned checkout (fixtures
     are never foreign-owned).
   Without the last one, D7 plus the simulation is red (1d).
4. **`unreadable` uses the fixed token for `$REV`**, and S0 carries the "HEAD unreadable" red.
   Before, three parse-only rows went red with the wrong cause.
5. **The skip counts moved.** H9 is in NEEDS_DEPTH, so shallow skips 3 (was 2) and export skips
   6 (was 5).
6. **No T1 was run.** It was not asked. The change touches only `test_issue_stamp`, and nothing
   else in `tests/` or `src/` sources the checker (READ: `grep -rl issue_stamp`). The `t1` arm
   above (cd `tests/`, `../src/xschem --nogui --pipe -q --script headless/test_issue_stamp.tcl`)
   is T1's own spelling.

### Decision: `foreign` is gone, and the rule is per stamp

**`foreign` is not a state any more.** A tree with a readable history is `full`. The checker then
skips one stamp's revision, by name, only when there is positive evidence that the revision cannot
be in that history. The evidence: **the history's earliest root commit was authored on a later day
than the stamp's `stamped=` date.**
- The author date (`%at`) is used because a rebase or filter-repo keeps it.
- The earliest root is used, never the latest.
- The day is read at **UTC-12**, the earliest calendar day that instant falls on anywhere, and the
  comparison is **strict**. A stamp has a date but no zone, so a root on the stamp's own day, in any
  reading, does not count as provably after it.

Every other unresolved revision in a full history stays red. The fail-closed fallback in the task
(revert `foreign`, re-init goes red) was not needed. The per-stamp rule is fail-closed on every
shape measured below.

**New state `unborn`**, handled exactly as `none`: git is not asked about revisions, and the
NEEDS_REPO rows skip by name. It is its own name, not `none`, so that H6 can check it against the
disk (an unborn HEAD) and the output says what happened. It needs **all three** of these pieces of
evidence:
- `rev-parse -q --verify HEAD` exits 1 quietly;
- `for-each-ref` lists no ref at all;
- `cat-file --batch-all-objects` shows no `commit` object.

A failed ancestry walk that does not meet all three is `unreadable`. MEASURED on git 2.53:
- a gutted `.git` whose branch still names a lost commit answers `HEAD` with exit 0 and the name;
- it answers `HEAD^{commit}` with exit 1 (the same as unborn), which is why the probe asks for `HEAD`;
- an orphan branch in a repository that has history has an unborn HEAD but still has refs and
  commits.

**Ancestry.** `git rev-list --max-parents=0 --format=%at HEAD` must succeed, in full *and* shallow
trees. A failure is `unreadable` unless it is `unborn`.
- `rev-list` is plumbing and replaces `log`: `log` obeys the caller's `log.*` config, and `git_in`
  keeps config on purpose (for D7).
- MEASURED: noshallow gives `Could not read <c1>` / `Failed to traverse parents` with exit 128. A
  corrupt c1 gives `loose object … is corrupt` with exit 128. The main tree answers in 0.10 s.

### What changed, by task item

| # | problem | change |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `foreign` failed open, and `git init` alone went red | The anchor is removed. The probe has the states `full`/`shallow`/`unborn`/`none`/`unreadable`, and `full` carries `begins` (the day at UTC-12). `rev_verdict rev stamped` returns `{verdict why}`, and the reason reaches the NOT VERIFIED line (`0056: tree=61af3692 (history begins 2026-09-18, after this stamp's 2026-09-17)`). Quotes are dated by their file's stamp. The CLI tail reads `full: history begins <day>, after them` or `unborn: no commits yet`. Suite changes are listed below the table. |
| 2 | H7 could not see a broken git check | H7 now compares `{skipped-set, git-consistency}`. The second column is red when `HAVE_GIT` is 0 but the state is `full`, `shallow` or `unborn`, since only a git that ran can produce those states. |
| 3 | Fixture build not sealed from ignore/attributes files and GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH | `g()` gains `-c core.excludesFile=/dev/null -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`, and every `git add` is `add -f`. `FIXTURE_ENV_SET` gains `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`. `FIXTURE_ENV_UNSET` gains `GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` (an env override of the protocol pin), `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`. **A hostile environment is planted around the build in every run:** an `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` whose `git/ignore` is `*` and whose `git/attributes` is `* working-tree-encoding=UTF-16`, plus a `GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH`. So the seal is exercised on this box every run, not on a tester's. The comment now lists what is sealed and was measured, names the one thing that was not measured (`GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` needs a root-owned `/etc/gitattributes` to plant), and names what is not claimed (`GIT_EXEC_PATH`). |
| 4 | A `%41` parent directory broke the file:// clone | `istamp_file_url` percent-encodes every UTF-8 byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9/._~-]`, and the URL is passed to `sh` as `$2`. MEASURED clone OK under `pct%41dir`, `sp ace`, `h#sh`, `br[x]`, `d$y`, `é`, `co:lon`, `q?m` and `pct%2541`. |
| 5 | The suite died on an unreadable issue file or a read-only checkout | (a) Every read of the real corpus goes through `istamp_read_issue`. B1 and B4 are now `check_needs` rows (caught) that name `NNNN:UNREADABLE`, and the D9 census names unreadable files instead of dying. (b) The scratch root is probed once. If `.scratch` cannot be written, the run's directories go to `$TMPDIR/istamp_<pid>_tmproot`, a `## scratch:` line says so, and the root is deleted at the end. The end-of-run sweep also removes a dead owner's `istamp_<pid>_tmproot` older than 300 s, and nothing else in TMPDIR. S20's driver write is caught (`DRIVER NOT WRITTEN`). If nothing at all can be written, row **X0** fails by name and RESULT is printed at once. |

**Suite changes for item 1.**
- `SUITE_ROOT_DAY` is a second, independent reading of the root day: the suite's own min, and
  `epoch − 12 h` formatted in UTC.
- `NEEDS_REPO {S0 S20c Q1 Q2}` is blocked in `none` and `unborn`.
- `NEEDS_DEPTH {G4 H9}` is blocked in `shallow`, `none` and `unborn`.
- `NEEDS_HISTORY {D9h}` is blocked in those states and also when **HLATE** is set (a full history
  that began after every stamp).
- S20b is split, and its HEAD half is **S20c** (NEEDS_REPO, a named skip).
- G4 is dated on the history's own first day, so it is refused in every full history, a re-init
  included.
- D9h asserts that the gate's skips equal the suite's own `D9_PREDATES` exactly (empty in an
  ordinary clone), with at least one stamp verified.
- H9 compares the real corpus with git directly, with four checks:
  - it adds `HLATE agrees`;
  - it adds `begins agrees`, meaning the suite's day equals the checker's;
  - every revision resolves or predates the history;
  - there is at least one stamp.
- H1 probes **11** fixtures. Fixture dates are pinned. The fixture `foreign` is renamed **reinit**
  and begins 2026-09-20. New fixtures:
  - `unborn` and `staged`;
  - `orphan`, which must be `unreadable`;
  - `late`: two roots, r1 authored `2026-06-15 01:00 +1400` (06-13 at UTC-12), r2 authored 07-01,
    both committed 08-01, plus their merge;
  - `noshallow`, which must be `unreadable`.
- New rows:
  - **H11**: the date rule on its boundaries, on `late`;
  - **H12**: `hrow unborn`.
- H8 is `hrow reinit`. H10 expects `{none {full 0 2026-09-20}}`.

### Deviations

1. **`unborn` is its own state name.** The task said "treat as `none`", and it behaves exactly as
   `none`. It needs three pieces of evidence, not only "HEAD does not resolve". An orphan branch in
   a repository that has history is `unreadable`, and H1 holds that.
2. **The day is read at UTC-12**, which is stricter than "the stamp's own precision". A re-init
   committed before 12:00 UTC on the day after the newest stamp reads as that stamp's day, so it is
   not provably later and goes red (INFERRED from the arithmetic; the day-of boundary is MEASURED on
   `late`, rows L2 and L3). Fail-closed was chosen over convenient.
3. The row count moved:
   - S20b's HEAD half is its own row, S20c;
   - G4 moved to NEEDS_DEPTH and is dated on the root's day;
   - D9h and H9 are redefined;
   - H11 and H12 are new;
   - 64 → 67.
4. A **permanent hostile plant** in the fixture build. The task asked for red-first with the
   refuter's recipes. The plant makes that red-first standing: it runs every time.
5. `log` is replaced by `rev-list` for the root walk (`log.*` config).
6. **The suite now deletes outside the checkout.** It removes a dead owner's
   `$TMPDIR/istamp_<pid>_tmproot` older than 300 s, and only that name.
7. Cost: 9.0 s → 13.2 s.

### Open (not fixed here)

- **A history re-created from scratch after the stamps** reads as a re-init, so its stamps are NOT
  VERIFIED by name, and green. Examples: an orphan squash committed today, or a filter-repo that
  also moves author dates. That is the rule working as designed, since there is no evidence to tell
  it apart (INFERRED, not measured). A root author date can be forged (INFERRED).
- **Redundant layers, each holding alone** (MEASURED, `logs/runs/sab_*`).
  - In `unborn_evidence`, removing the refs check alone stayed green, and so did removing the
    objects check alone. Removing both reddened H1.
  - Removing `core.excludesFile` alone stayed green, and so did removing `add -f` alone. Removing
    both reddened the fixture rows.
  - So neither layer of either pair is held by a row on its own; only the pair is.
- `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is set but unmeasured: planting `/etc/gitattributes` needs root.
- These carry over:
  - `summarize_all` still drops `skip:` lines (D12);
  - the offline blob:none `quote=` latent limit stands;
  - `HAVE_GIT` still depends on `timeout`.
- Not touched by this stage:
  - `/tmp/xschem_emergencysave_*` directories;
  - the `:99` state (the user killed pids 1209133/1209045 during this stage; this crew ran no
    devdisplay command).

**Crew** S1-fix3. **Date** 2026-09-18. **Tree** `fluid-editing` at `616110a6` (the driver's D14
commit). The two files were edited in place in the main tree and are not committed; the driver
holds git. Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix3/`. Shapes are in `shapes/`, the final battery's
logs in `logs/final/` and `logs/runs/`, and sabotage copies in `sab/`. S1-fix2's bytes
(`4f990c5e`/`fc98eca4`) are kept in `pre/`, and every "S1-fix2 bytes" column below ran on them.
Tags: **MEASURED**, **READ**, **INFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**.

**Final md5:**
- `issue_stamp.tcl 2c6436ef1681bd538fb53daa7d2ce6c3`
- `test_issue_stamp.tcl bf814c4fb5b0b28926a39b0e665f1462`

before and after (`logs/final/md5_{before,after}.txt`), and in every shape on every run (the `md5=`
column of every log line).

**Diff against S1-fix2:**

- suite: +336/−250

The suite has 67 rows → **71**: S9b, H13, H14 and H15 are new, and H11 is redefined. The self-test
has 17 parser cases → **19**. Cost, MEASURED back to back in the `xschem` clone, two runs each:
S1-fix2 12.9 / 13.3 s, final 19.0 / 18.8 s, so about **+6 s** on a T1 of about 380 s.
A per-line profile attributes it to three places:
- fixture build plus H1 (13 probes): 4.6 s;
- the H13/H14 block (10 gate runs): 3.1 s;
- H15: 1.7 s.

### What changed, by D14 item

| D14 item | change |
|---|---|
| **No exemption in a history with commits** | `rev_verdict rev` loses its `stamped` argument and the date rule. `full` and `unreadable` never skip; only `none`, `unborn` and shallow's unresolved revisions do. The probe no longer computes `begins`; `full` carries `roots` instead. The suite drops `SUITE_ROOT_DAY`, `HLATE`, `D9_PREDATES`, `G4_DAY` and the `late` fixture. NEEDS_HISTORY merges into NEEDS_DEPTH `{G4 H9 D9h}`, since the only thing that told them apart was HLATE. G4 goes back to a fixed `stamped=2026-09-17`. |
| **The red explains itself** | New `istamp::upstream_root` = `7fe79fb2bf4230e3e1c52432082ccba804e868fc`. `lacks_upstream` is 1 exactly when the state is `full` and the probe's roots lack it. It is the single check and **nothing that decides a verdict calls it**. The walk is `git --no-replace-objects rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`, one exec, which is both the completeness proof and the roots. `upstream_note` appends the cause and the cure to a `does not resolve` problem, and to a quote problem whose revision is `bad`. `history_why` appends a `NOTE:`, worded so that it stays true in an orphan squash whose stamps still resolve. |
| **Exit code, not stderr** | New `istamp::git_run`: `exec … 2>/dev/null`, so only the exit code decides. On failure (not a timeout, rc 124) it re-runs with `2>@1` to fetch git's words, and `git_words` puts the first `fatal:`/`error:` line first. `git_in` = with words; `git_q` = quiet (`rev_exists`, `quote_holds`, which fail routinely and never print the words). The suite's `HAVE_GIT` and H0's `rev-parse --local-env-vars` also get `2>/dev/null`. |
| **Calendar** | `parse_stamp` refuses a `stamped=` that is not a real Gregorian day, year 0001-9999 (`is_calendar_day`, plain arithmetic). `clock scan` is not used because it rolls 2026-02-30 over to 03-02 (MEASURED, Tcl 8.6.17). The self-test gains `0000-00-00` and `2026-02-30` as known-bad. |
| **Rows** | **S9b**: calendar known-bads and known-goods. **D9h** now asserts `{1 1 {}}` (every stamp verified, **skip set empty**); its failing D9 prints the first three problems, indented. **H9**: every stamped revision resolves, asked of git directly. **H11**: (ii) and (iii) in the full fixture. **H13**: (i) and (iv) on five full histories. **H14**: the wording and its verdict-invariance. **H15**: warnings are not failures. **H8**/**H10**/**H1** are re-expected. |

**Fixtures (13).** `late` is removed and three are added:
- `graft`: full plus c3 (authored 2026-09-20), then `git replace --graft HEAD`;
- `orphanc`: full with an orphan branch committed (sq, 2026-09-20) and checked out;
- `rootdate`: full's history rewritten with only the root's author date moved to 2026-09-20.

H1 now also names each full fixture's roots as the probe found them: `{c1 f1 c1 sq x1}`. graft's is **c1, not c3**.

**H14's design** (READ): one corpus is run on five full histories under **two readings** of the
project's first commit: the real one (in no fixture) and c1 (in full's and graft's stored history).
The row asserts three things:
- the note counts under c1 are `{0 3 0 2 3}`;
- the note counts under the real anchor are `{2 3 2 2 3}`;
- the verdict `{state problems skipped verified}` is **identical under both readings in all five**.

**H15's plant**: a `[core] fsyncObjectFiles = true` global config **and** `GIT_TRACE=2`. Its first
column proves that the plant really reaches stderr.

The problem as the CLI prints it in the re-init shape (MEASURED, `final_reinit_cli.log`):
```
history: full -- a full clone: every stamped revision must resolve -- NOTE: HEAD's history does not contain the project's first commit 7fe79fb2, so this is not an ordinary clone of the project (a download put under git, an orphan squash or a rewrite?); any stamped revision that does not resolve here is reported, and a real clone is the cure
ISSUE-STAMP: 0056: tree=61af3692 does not resolve to a commit in this repo. The whole value of the stamp is that `git show 61af3692:<file>` recovers every coordinate in the file; a revision that does not resolve recovers nothing. HEAD's history does not contain the project's first commit (7fe79fb2), so this is not an ordinary clone of the project -- most likely a downloaded copy put under git (`git init` and a commit), a squash onto an orphan branch, or a rewritten history.  Run this in a real clone: `git clone <url>`, without --depth.
```
The suite's D9 prints the first three of these under its FAIL line, plus `...and 7 more: tclsh
tests/headless/issue_stamp.tcl gate lists them all`.

### Deviations

1. **NEEDS_HISTORY is merged into NEEDS_DEPTH.** Without HLATE the two lists were blocked in
   identical states. The shallow and export skip sets are unchanged (`G4 D9h H9`; `S0 S20c G4 Q1
   Q2 D9h H9`).
2. **Row (i) is asserted twice.** It is on the real corpus (D9h's third column, `{}`), and across
   five full fixture histories (H13's skip column). No separate D9s row was added, because it would
   have to skip in shallow, where the skip set is legitimately non-empty. That would have changed
   shallow's named skips, which D14 keeps as they were.
3. **(iv) is fixtures AND recipes.** The fixtures `graft`, `orphanc` and `rootdate` are in H1, H13
   and H14. The refuter's three recipes were measured on real clones (the red-first table).
4. **`late` is removed.** It existed only to pin the date rule's readings. With it goes the one
   fixture whose history had **two** roots, so no row now probes a multi-root history. The
   project's own history has one root (MEASURED by S1-fix), and `lacks_upstream` reads a list, so
   a second root can only add an entry, never hide one (READ).
5. **Git's words are fetched by re-running a failed call with stderr kept.** This avoids temp
   files (a read-only TMPDIR is a measured shape) and Tcl-8.6-only `chan pipe`. Routine failures
   (`rev_exists`) do not re-run.
6. **The wording row (H14) goes red when the wording check is removed.** That is the text change
   being detected. The verdict-level proof is the CLI rc/problem columns above, plus H14's own
   third column and H13 staying green under that sabotage.
7. **Cost**: +6 s (above).

## S1-fix4

**Crew** S1-fix4. **Date** 2026-09-18. **Tree** `fluid-editing` at `caa110ba` (the driver's D15
commit). The two files were edited in place in the main tree and are not committed; the driver
holds git. Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix4/`. Shapes are in `shapes/`, logs in `logs/`
(`logs/runs/<tag>.<arm>.log` for each run), and the scripts are `run.sh`, `inst.sh`, `plant.sh`,
`rf1-4.sh`, `rfinal.sh`, `arms.sh`, `hermetic.sh`, `misc.sh`, `defects.sh`, `sab.py` and `score.sh`.
S1-fix3's bytes (`2c6436ef`/`bf814c4f`) are kept in `pre/` and `files/pre/`, and every "S1-fix3
bytes" column below ran on them. Tags: **MEASURED**, **READ**, **INFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**.

**Final md5:**
- `issue_stamp.tcl 6f64991118c6407b43fdef2c852419e4`
- `test_issue_stamp.tcl 41adf73392b80a7517ce42c0440f0062`

These are frozen in `files/final/`, and the main tree was md5-checked equal to them at the end.
Every "final" result below ran on these bytes (the `md5=6f649911 41adf733` column of each log line).

**Diff against S1-fix3:**
- checker: +368/−47
- suite: +362/−57

The suite has 71 rows → **76**: Q3, Q4, H16, H17 and H18 are new; H1, H6, H9, H13 and H14 are
re-expected. The self-test has 19 cases → **34**, adding known answers for the revision grammar
and the two unread-text detectors.

**Cost.** MEASURED side by side in this session: S1-fix3's bytes take 18.9-19.2 s, the final bytes
29.2-30.2 s. That is **+10 s** on a T1 of about 380 s. The cause is also MEASURED: `/usr/bin/timeout`
on this box is **uutils coreutils 0.8.0**, which costs a flat 100 ms per call whatever its flags
(`timeout 30 true` takes 0.10 s from bash; Tcl `exec git --version` takes 0.8 ms bare and 102 ms
under `timeout`). The new rows and fixtures add about 40 wrapped git calls, and the ancestry
question adds one call per resolved revision. That 100 ms taxes every exec of every suite in the
tree, so it is recorded here for the driver rather than worked around in these two files.

### Measured first (items 4 and 5, before any code)

- **Item 4** (MEASURED, main tree at `caa110ba`). All 10 stamps name `61af3692`, and `git
  merge-base --is-ancestor 61af3692 HEAD` gives rc 0 for every one. No stamp names a
  non-ancestor, so the ancestor rule is implemented as D15 states it. The "reachable from some ref"
  fallback was **not needed**.
- **Item 5** (MEASURED, all 1050 issue files):
  - 0 stamp-shaped lines off column 0. The 7 lines with a bold "stamp" are prose such as
    `**Stamp the record** with a token`, and the detector's pattern does not match them.
  - 0 `~~~` fences.
  - 84 indented backtick fences, none carrying `quote=`.
  - 0 fence lines carrying `quote` in any case.
  - The corpus's one `quote=` is 0993's C in prose (`quote=!quote;`), in a file with no stamp.
  - The real corpus therefore stays at 0 problems. **Nothing had to be hidden.**

### What changed, by D15 item

| D15 item | change |
|---|---|
| **1. shallow is about HEAD** | `history_probe`: `shallow` needs `--is-shallow-repository = true` **and** a root of HEAD's `--no-replace-objects` walk whose **stored object carries a `parent` line** (`shallow_boundaries`, one `cat-file --batch` exec, run only when the flag is up, so a full clone pays nothing). Otherwise the state is `full`, and its `why` says so: *"git calls this repository shallow, but nothing its shallow file names cuts HEAD's history, which is complete back to its root commit 7fe79fb2"*. The shallow `why` names the boundary: *"HEAD's history ends at the shallow boundary caa110ba"*. A failure to show the roots is `unreadable`, never a skip. |
| **2. lstat** | `.git` is detected with `file lstat`. Only ENOENT or ENOTDIR is `none`; any other lstat failure is `unreadable`, and so is a `.git` that exists as a link but does not resolve (*"a symbolic link to `…`, which does not exist"*). A gitfile naming a missing directory was already `unreadable` (git refuses it) and is now held by a row. |
| **3. no corpus text as an option** | New `rev_token` is the one grammar for `tree=`, `super=` and **`quote=`** (7-40 hex, at least one a-f). The gate refuses a non-token `quote=` by name **before git is asked** (*"quote=X is not a revision … nothing else in an issue file is ever handed to git"*). `rev_exists`, the new `rev_is_ancestor` and `quote_holds` all pass `--end-of-options` before the value (MEASURED on git 2.53 for `cat-file -e`, `merge-base --is-ancestor` and `show`: an `--output=` value becomes *"Not a valid object name"*, rc 128, and nothing is written). |
| **4. ancestor of HEAD** | `rev_verdict`: `cat-file -e` (resolves?) and then `merge-base --is-ancestor --end-of-options <rev> HEAD`. The outcomes: 1 → `ok`; 0 → `bad notancestor` in `full`/`unreadable`, or a skip by name in `shallow` (*"resolves, but is not reached above the shallow boundary"*); any other answer → `bad noanswer`. `rev_problem` words each, and appends `upstream_note` when HEAD's history lacks `7fe79fb2` (wording only, as in D14). A **quote=** of a non-ancestor is refused before `git show`, which would find it in the store and pass it. All three predicates read the history **as stored**: `--no-replace-objects`, plus the legacy grafts file off (see deviation 2). |
| **5. unread text is a problem** | `fence_scan` is `find_blocks`' parser, and it also records where each fence opened and whether it closed. `md_strip` removes markdown containers (indent, `>`, list markers, heading hashes). `stray_stamps` flags any line whose content, once stripped, is stamp-shaped but which `find_stamp` does not read (indented, blockquote, list, heading, or not spelled `**STAMP:**`); it runs on **every** file. `stray_quotes` flags any fence line carrying a quote attribute that no verified block came from, naming the cause: indented, `~~~`, never closed, inside another fenced block, blockquote or list, a key not written `quote=`, or a file with no stamp. Both run **before** the revision question, so they are named in every history state. |

**New suite rows** (all MEASURED green in the main tree, and red on the recipes and sabotages below):
- **H16**: `offpath` (main cloned whole, then **another** branch fetched `--depth 1`) scores exactly
  H2's full vector. A bogus stamp is RED in `eshallow` (an empty shallow file) and in `reachroot` (a
  `--depth 2` clone that lists the true root).
- **H17**: `quote=--output=<file>` is a named problem. `quote_holds`, `rev_exists` and
  `rev_is_ancestor`, handed that value **directly** (past the gate's grammar), answer
  `{0 0 -1}`, and **no file is written**.
- **H18**: in `amend` (full + `commit --allow-empty` + `reset --soft HEAD~1`), `tree=am` is RED, a
  `quote=am` is RED although `git show am:a.txt` finds the text, and c1 is verified. In `shamend`
  (the same on the shallow clone), am2 is NOT VERIFIED by name and c2 is verified.
- **Q3**: seven files. An indented fence, a `~~~` fence, a never-closed fence, a fence inside another
  block, a blockquoted fence and a quote in an unstamped file each give exactly one problem naming
  its cause. 0993's shape (prose and code containing `quote=`) gives none. The row runs in every
  history state.
- **Q4**: five files. An indented stamp, a blockquoted stamp, `**stamp:**` and a list-item stamp each
  give one problem naming its cause. S16's mid-sentence mention and 1317's `**Stamp the record**`
  give none.
- **H1**: 13 → **19** fixtures, adding `offpath`, `eshallow` and `reachroot` (must be `full`),
  `dangling` and `gitfile` (must be `unreadable`), and `grafts` (full + c3 + a legacy
  `.git/info/grafts` making c3 a root: stored root **c1**).
- **H6**: the disk evidence now reads HEAD's own commit out of the ref files. A shallow file that
  lists HEAD is `shallow` (a `--depth 1` clone). Any other shallow file is `shallowrepo`, which
  admits `full` or `shallow`: whether HEAD's walk reaches a boundary is in no file, and H1/H16 hold
  the probe to that instead. H6 would otherwise have gone red in every fetchd/ciflow-shaped clone.
  The disk evidence also uses lstat.
- **H9**: asks resolution **and** ancestry of git directly. The census is caught, so an error
  names itself instead of killing the suite.
- **H13/H14**: in the orphan squash, c1 is now refused. It is in the store and on `main`, and it
  is not in HEAD's history: `{full 3 0 0}`, with note counts `{0 3 0 3 3}` / `{2 3 2 3 3}`. The
  verdict is still identical under both readings.

### Deviations

1. **Item 1's test is "a root whose stored object names a parent", and D15's other disjunct
   ("listed in the shallow file") is dropped.** MEASURED: `git clone --depth 2` of a two-commit
   history is flagged shallow, and its shallow file **lists c1, the true root**, which has no parent
   and cuts nothing. The literal disjunct would call that complete history `shallow` and skip a
   bogus stamp. Sabotage **B2** (the disjunct added back) is exactly that: H1 (`reachroot` →
   shallow) and H16 (`reachroot` bogus `{0 1 0}`) go red. With replace refs and the grafts file
   off, a walk root that has a stored parent line exists only where the shallow file cut the walk,
   so every real boundary is still caught. The deviation is in the fail-closed direction.
2. **The legacy grafts file is switched off for every checker git call** (`GIT_GRAFT_FILE`
   pointing at `/dev/null/istamp-no-grafts`; git is silent on ENOTDIR). D15 does not ask for this.
   Item 4 needs it: MEASURED, `--no-replace-objects` does **not** disable `.git/info/grafts`, so a
   grafts line could make a commit that no clone carries look like an ancestor of HEAD (a fail-open)
   or cut HEAD's walk. A stranger's clone never receives a grafts file, so "as stored" is the
   stranger's view. Consequence (MEASURED): S1-fix3's "legacy grafts file makes the NOTE appear on
   a green run" is gone. The shape is `full`, ALL PASS 76, with no NOTE. Sabotage **A3** (grafts
   honoured) turns H1's `grafts` root into c3.
3. **The ancestor rule covers `quote=` as well as `tree=`.** They share `rev_verdict`, and a
   non-ancestor quote has the same stranger failure. H18's third column holds it (sabotage **A4**).
4. **`stray_quotes` also runs on files with no stamp.** In such a file no quote is ever verified,
   so a `quote=` fence there is exactly the silent pass D15.5 forbids. MEASURED impact today: 0
   lines. The known negative is 0993's prose.
5. **Behaviour changes in shapes that were already measured** (all MEASURED, all in the
   fail-closed direction):
   - **The orphan squash** (`checkout --orphan sq && commit`) was ALL PASS 71 on S1-fix3's bytes.
     It is now **RED**: D9 D9h H9, CLI 10 problems, each explained by the note. The stamps
     resolve in the store and are not in the squash's history, which is D15.4's case.
   - **Corrupt shallow** (`.git/shallow` deleted) was 4 reds. It is now **12**: G2 Q1 Q2 A2 A3
     A4 N1 N2 D9 D9h H6 H9. `merge-base` cannot walk the broken history, so a revision that
     resolves is `noanswer` → red instead of verified. The state is `unreadable`, which is red by
     design, and RESULT is reached.
   - **Dangling `.git`** was `none` (64 + 7 skips, bogus stamp green). It is now `unreadable`: 13
     named reds, RESULT reached, CLI 10 problems.
6. **The first red-first pass ran on intermediate bytes** (`19b97094`/`ae6da854`). They differ from
   the frozen bytes only in the full-with-flag `why` sentence, one comment, and D9's census being

### Every git exec in the final bytes, and how its arguments are protected (READ, `grep` of both files)

The checker has **two** exec lines that run git, both in `git_run`. They are `exec timeout $t_git
git -C $dir {*}$args 2>/dev/null` and the words re-run `… 2>@1`. `$dir` is always an absolute,
normalised path derived from the checker's own location or from `init <dir>` (never from the
corpus), and it is the value of `-C`, not a positional. Every call goes through `git_in`/`git_q`:

| line | call | corpus value? | protection |
|---|---|---|---|
| 464 | `rev-parse --show-prefix --is-shallow-repository` | no | literals only |
| 491 | `--no-replace-objects rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` | no | literals |
| 546 | `--no-replace-objects cat-file --batch << roots` | no | the roots come from git's own walk, checked `^[0-9a-f]{40}([0-9a-f]{24})?$`, and go through **stdin**, not argv |
| 571, 578, 584 | `rev-parse -q --verify HEAD`, `for-each-ref`, `cat-file --batch-all-objects` | no | literals |
| 1079 | `rev_exists`: `--no-replace-objects cat-file -e --end-of-options <rev>^{commit}` | **tree= / quote=** | `rev_token` grammar at parse or gate time, then `--end-of-options` |
| 1090 | `rev_is_ancestor`: `--no-replace-objects merge-base --is-ancestor --end-of-options <rev> HEAD` | **tree= / quote=** | the same two locks |
| 1109 | `quote_holds`: `--no-replace-objects show --end-of-options <rev>:<path>` | **quote= and path=** | the same two locks; the argument begins with the hex revision, so `path=` can never lead it |

The checker's two `grep` execs are not git. `assert_eval` passes `pat=` after `--`, and `path=` is
joined under the repository. `report` passes no corpus value.

The suite's execs:
- `HAVE_GIT`: `git --version`.
- `istamp_test_rev`: `rev-parse --short=N HEAD`, where N comes from a literal list.
- Q1: `show --end-of-options ${REV}:tests/banner_rule.tcl`. `$REV` is HEAD's own abbreviation or
  the fixed token, never corpus text; `--end-of-options` was added for uniformity.
- H0: `rev-parse --local-env-vars`.
- H15: `-C <fixture> rev-parse HEAD`.
- The fixture `sh` script: its arguments are this run's scratch paths and URLs.
- S20: its driver runs under `tclsh`, not git.

**No corpus text reaches any of them.**

### Open (not fixed here)

- **Redundant layer, measured (I4).** `--end-of-options` on `rev_exists` and `rev_is_ancestor` is
  not held by any row on its own. The grammar blocks every non-hex value before them, and neither
  `cat-file -e` nor `merge-base` has an option that writes. Only `quote_holds`' lock is held
  alone (H17, I2).
- **Two fail-closed branches are exercised by no row:** `noanswer` (the revision resolves and
  `merge-base` errors), reached only in the corrupt-shallow shape; and `shallow_boundaries` failing
  (→ `unreadable`). Both go red, never skip (READ).
- **`assert=`'s `path=` is not confined to the repository.** An absolute path makes `grep` read
  outside the repository. It is read-only and bounded at 60 s, and not a git call, so it is out of
  D15's scope (READ).
- **`/usr/bin/timeout` is uutils 0.8.0, at a flat 100 ms per call** (MEASURED). It is the whole of
  this suite's cost increase and taxes every suite in the tree. Replacing it is outside these two
  files and is recorded for the driver.
- **In a real shallow clone every revision-level defect is still NOT VERIFIED rather than
  refused.** This is the stated limit, unchanged, and H2/H16/H18 prove the refusal on fixtures in
  every shape.
- **Carried over:**
  - `summarize_all` drops `skip:` lines (D12);
  - `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is unmeasured (it needs root);
  - `HAVE_GIT` depends on `timeout`;
  - the offline blob:none `quote=` limit is latent (0 quote blocks in the corpus).

### What changed, by D16 item

| D16 item | change |
|---|---|
| **1. no exec with corpus text** | `assert_eval` no longer execs anything. `confine_path` checks the path, then `assert_scan` / `scan_dir` / `scan_file` read the files in Tcl. `pat=` is a **literal**: its UTF-8 bytes are searched in each file's bytes, and a hit is counted once per line that contains it (grep -rn's count). Recursion follows grep -r: hidden files are read, a symbolic link met below path= is not followed, and a device, FIFO or socket there is skipped. A file with a NUL byte that matches is **unevaluable, by name**. That is the verdict grep 3.12 produced: it reports "binary file matches" on stderr, which the old exec read as a failure (MEASURED). A path= that is itself neither file nor directory is refused, because a FIFO would block. An empty `pat=` is refused. The scan has its own 60 s clock budget (`t_scan`), since no `timeout` can wrap Tcl. `quote_holds` now sends `<rev>:<path>` to `git cat-file --batch` **on stdin**, not on argv; a name that is not a blob is "not a file at that revision". `rev_exists`, `rev_is_ancestor` and the new `rev_type` each check `rev_token` themselves, as well as passing `--end-of-options`. |
| **2. path= confined** | `path_shape_problem`: refused when empty, absolute, starting with `-`, or holding a `..` component. The check is by string, before anything is looked at. `resolve_in` walks the components from the checkout's real root, by string and `lstat`, never with `file join`. A `..` at the root, or a link whose target is outside, answers `out` before anything beyond the root is lstat'ed. Absolute link targets are accepted only when they name the root or a path under it by string. The same shape rule applies to a quote= block's path=, and it is refused before git is asked. |
| **3. shallow = the store cannot answer** | When the flag is up, `history_probe` walks HEAD again with `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` set to the **empty string**, plus `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, through the new `git_env`, which sets these after the scrub. If that walk completes, the tree is `full` with the true roots. It is `shallow` only if the walk exits **128** *and* a boundary sits on HEAD's honoured walk (`shallow_boundaries`); any other outcome is `unreadable`. In `full`, `rev_is_ancestor` also disregards the shallow file. MEASURED necessary: in fdself/fdanc, `merge-base --is-ancestor 61af3692 HEAD` answers 1 when the file is honoured and 0 when it is disregarded. |
| **4. a present non-commit is RED** | When a revision does not resolve to a commit, `rev_verdict` asks `rev_type` (`cat-file -t`, no lazy fetch). A blob or tree gives `bad {notcommit <type>}` in every state, worded "names a blob in this repository, not a commit, so it does not resolve to a commit…". A tag is not decided there, because its commit may lie beyond the depth. |
| **5. CommonMark fences, stamps by content** | `fence_scan`: a fence is 3+ backticks or 3+ tildes indented up to 3 spaces, and it closes only on the **same character, at least as long**, indented up to 3 spaces, with only blanks after. `~~~` fences and indented fences are containers (their contents are text), but only a column-0 backtick fence is **read**. `stamp_shaped` strips emphasis (`*`/`_` runs and `<strong> <b> <em> <i>`). A line is then stamp-shaped when it begins with `stamp:` and either was written in any emphasis, or is followed by `` `v1 `` (the backtick optional). It is still anchored at the head of the line's content, so S16's mid-sentence mention stays prose. |

### Mechanism for item 3, measured on git 2.53 (`logs` of the probe commands in this session)

| spelling | fdself | fdanc | --depth 1 | --depth 20 | partial+shallow | full |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| honoured (no override) | root = HEAD | root = 634b0cec | root = HEAD | root = 424d4fe9 | root = HEAD | 7fe79fb2 |
| `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE=` (empty) — **chosen** | 7fe79fb2 | 7fe79fb2 | rc 128 | rc 128 | rc 128 | 7fe79fb2 |
| `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE=/dev/null/none` | 7fe79fb2 | 7fe79fb2 | rc 128 | rc 128 | rc 128 | 7fe79fb2 |
| `git --shallow-file ""` | 7fe79fb2 | 7fe79fb2 | rc 128 | rc 128 | rc 128 | 7fe79fb2 |
| `git --shallow-file=` | rc 129 (usage) | rc 129 | rc 129 | rc 129 | rc 129 | rc 129 |

**Why the empty string** (READ in git's `is_repository_shallow`; the rest INFERRED from that):
- The empty path is git's own "no shallow file" indicator. fetch-pack passes it, and git tests
  `!*path` before it opens anything.
- A git that ever ignored the variable would honour `.git/shallow` again, call a real shallow clone
  `full`, and redden its stamps. That failure is loud.
- `/dev/null/none` works only because an unopenable file reads as none. A git that died on it
  instead would turn every flagged full history `shallow`, which is a skip.
- `--shallow-file` is internal and undocumented, and its `=` form is already a usage error.

**The caller cannot steer it.** `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` is in `git_scrub`, and `git_env` sets the value
after the scrub. Row H21 plants the caller's `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE=""` around a real shallow fixture,
which must stay `shallow`. It also plants a shallow file listing HEAD around the full fixture,
together with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=0`, which must stay `full` with c1 an ancestor. Both variables
must be restored exactly. Sabotage G3 (the variable removed from the scrub) and G4 (the restore
removed) redden H21.

**Lazy fetch.** In a `--depth 1 --filter=blob:none` clone, the disregarding walk made **no**
promisor fetch (MEASURED: objects 8 → 8, and no `run_command` in `GIT_TRACE`). `cat-file` of an
absent full-length name **does** start `git fetch` there (MEASURED). That is why `rev_type` passes
`GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`.

### RED before → after, the refuter's recipes (MEASURED; S1-fix4 bytes vs frozen final bytes; scratch clone `shapes/xschem`)

### Every exec and every open in the final bytes, with provenance (READ, `grep -n` of both files)

Checker:

| where | exec / open | words | corpus-derived word? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `git_env` (the only git exec; its failure words re-run is the same line with `2>@1`) | `exec timeout $t_git git -C $dir {*}$args 2>/dev/null` | `$dir` is the checker's own repo or `init <dir>`; `$args` per call site below | only as listed below |
| `history_probe`, `unborn_evidence` | rev-parse, rev-list (honoured and `noshallow`), for-each-ref, `cat-file --batch-all-objects` | literals | no |
| `shallow_boundaries` | `cat-file --batch << roots` | stdin: root SHAs from git's own walk, checked hex | no |
| `rev_exists` | `cat-file -e --end-of-options ${rev}^{commit}` | `rev` | **yes, a revision**: `rev_token` checked in this proc and in the gate/parser, behind `--end-of-options` |
| `rev_type` | `cat-file -t --end-of-options $rev` | `rev` | **yes, a revision**: the same two locks |
| `rev_is_ancestor` | `merge-base --is-ancestor --end-of-options $rev HEAD` | `rev` | **yes, a revision**: the same two locks |
| `quote_holds` | `cat-file --batch << "${rev}:${path}\n"` | argv: literals only; **stdin**: the quote's revision and path | on stdin only, never argv (Tcl parses nothing in a `<<` value) |
| `report` | `exec timeout $t_grep /usr/bin/grep -rnoE {<literal ERE>} [file join $repo <literal dir>]` | literals and the repo path | no |
| `read_file` | `open $path r` | the issue files' absolute paths (the names match `^[0-9]{4}-`), the baseline | a name from the directory listing, absolute, so never a pipe |
| `scan_file` | `open $f r` | absolute paths from the walk, checked to begin with `/` | no pipe possible |

Suite execs:
- `HAVE_GIT` (`git --version`);
- S20's driver (`tclsh <scratch>/drv.tcl`);
- the fixture `sh -c` (scratch paths and file:// URLs);
- H0 (`rev-parse --local-env-vars`);
- H15 (`git -C <fixture> rev-parse HEAD`);
- the git calls routed through the checker's `git_in`: `istamp_test_rev` (`rev-parse --short=N HEAD`) and Q1 (`show --end-of-options $REV:tests/banner_rule.tcl`, where `$REV` is HEAD's own name or the fixed token).

Every suite `open` writes a scratch path. **No word from an issue file reaches any argv except a
`rev_token`-validated revision behind `--end-of-options`.**

### Deviations

1. **`pat=` is literal, not grep's basic regular expression.** D16 left the choice to "the spec's
   `pat=` semantics". The spec calls it "one whitespace-free token", and the refuter's recipes had
   to be "judged as a literal pattern".
   - Literal matching also cannot hang: a BRE with back-references in Tcl's engine can, and
     no watchdog can interrupt a busy Tcl loop.
   - MEASURED: the corpus's one block has no character a BRE reads specially, and 84/84 benign
     pairs give identical counts.
   - A future `pat=` written as a regex would change meaning. `doc/claude/specs/issue_stamp.md` §4
     should say "literal" (not my file; for the driver).
2. **The binary-file rule keeps grep 3.12's verdict** (unevaluable, by name), keyed on a NUL byte
   only. grep's encoding-error test depends on the locale and is not reproduced.
3. **Also refused**, fail-closed (none is in the corpus): an empty `pat=`; a path= that is neither
   file nor directory; an absolute link target that reaches the checkout only through another
   link.
4. **quote= gained the path shape rule and moved to stdin.** D16 names assert='s path=; I applied
   the same "no corpus word on argv" rule to quote=. A quote naming a tree (directory) is now "not
   a file at that revision", where `git show` printed a listing. Row H17's third column holds the
   shape rule.
5. **`shallow` needs two pieces of evidence**: the disregarding walk exits 128 *and* a boundary
   sits on HEAD's honoured walk. Anything else is `unreadable`, never a skip.
6. **H6's disk evidence no longer tells a `--depth 1` clone apart.** Any shallow file is now
   `shallowrepo` and admits `full` or `shallow`. The old "lists HEAD ⇒ shallow" is exactly the
   fdself shape, and nothing in plain files says whether the store holds what lies beyond a
   boundary. H1 and H19 hold the probe on fixtures, and in a real `--depth 1` clone D9 would
   catch a misread.
7. **The `notcommit` wording applies in every state**, not only shallow. In `full` it was already
   red as "does not resolve"; the words now name the type. This is part of the cost (above).
8. **The xschem and T1 arms use the main tree's `src/xschem`** (built 09-17), as S1-fix4 and its
   refuter did. The shapes were not built.

### Open (not fixed here)

- **Redundant layer I2.** Removing `rev_token` from `rev_exists` stays green; the gate's grammar and
  `--end-of-options` hold. The lock is doubled on purpose, not held alone by a row.
- **Branches no row exercises:**
  - the disregarding walk failing with an exit other than 128 (→ `unreadable`);
  - that walk failing with no boundary on HEAD's walk (→ `unreadable`);
  - an empty `pat=` (refused).

  All three are fail-closed (READ).
- **The spelling choice is not testable by a row.** Today `""` and `/dev/null/none` behave
  identically (MEASURED); the choice rests on the failure direction argued above.
- **Q6 makes `out/` mode 000 for its length.** A run killed inside that row leaves a corpse the
  sweep cannot delete. The failure direction is "a leftover survives".
- **Cost is +11 s.** Restricting `rev_type` to `shallow` would save about 2-3 s, at the price of the
  type-naming words in `full` (INFERRED from the profile). This is the driver's call.
- **Carried over:**
  - `summarize_all` drops `skip:` lines (D12);
  - `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is unmeasured;
  - `HAVE_GIT` depends on `timeout`;
  - `quote_holds` in an online blob:none clone may still lazily fetch the quoted blob (latent: 0
    quote blocks in the corpus);
  - uutils `timeout` costs 100 ms per exec.

### What changed, by D18 item

| D18 item | change |
|---|---|
| **1. non-UTF-8 names in the assert= scan** | `assert_eval` runs the confinement and the scan inside `in_bytes`, which sets Tcl's system encoding to **iso8859-1** (every byte one character and back) for that one call, and puts it back exactly, whatever happens. `glob` then returns the directory's own bytes, and `lstat`/`open` are handed exactly those. The checkout root enters as the bytes the system encoding made of it (`as_bytes $repo [encoding system]`); `path=` and `pat=` enter as **UTF-8**, the corpus's own encoding, so `path=src/café` names the same directory in every locale. Every path a sentence names goes through `bytes_shown` (UTF-8 if valid, else `\xNN`). **The fail-closed fallback stays**: `scan_dir`'s `if {[catch {file lstat $p st}]} { continue }` is now an error, *"cannot look at `…`, which its directory lists (…) -- what it holds cannot be counted"*, so an entry that still cannot be looked at makes the assertion unevaluable, by name. |
| **2. assert= the parser does not read** | `stray_quotes` became `stray_attrs {num text scan {keys {quote assert}}}`: every fence line carrying `quote=` **or** `assert=` that no READ block came from is one problem, with the same causes (indented, blockquote, list item, heading, `~~~`, never closed, inside another block, not lowercase), and *"in a file with no **STAMP:** line, where no assert= is ever evaluated"* for an unstamped file. The gate calls it on both branches. `stray_quotes` remains, as the quote= half. |
| **3. not a regular file** | `not_regular` (lstat): anything but a regular file is *"is a symbolic link (to `…`), not a regular file"* / *"is a fifo, not a regular file"* / *"cannot be looked at (…)"*, and **is never opened**. The gate asks it before `read_file` for every issue file. `load_baseline` uses lstat instead of `file exists` (which followed a link), returns `{-1 {} why}` for a baseline that is not a regular file or cannot be read, and the gate answers *"BASELINE UNUSABLE: … -- it is not opened … so the gate refuses to run"*. `report` skips and counts such files instead of opening them. **Also** (deviation 2): `issues_dir_problem` walks `doc/claude/issues` from the checkout's real root with `resolve_in`, and a corpus directory that leads out through a link is *"CORPUS OUTSIDE THE CHECKOUT: …"*, with nothing in it read. |
| **4. md_strip linear** | One `regexp` with no capture matches the whole run of containers, then one `string range`. MEASURED 1 MB of `>` in 5 ms and 1 MB of `- ` in 4 ms (Tcl 8.6.17), and **identical to the old loop on 200 000 random strings** over the container characters (`mdprobe.tcl`), and on all 164 792 lines of the real corpus (`diff_parse.tcl`). |
| **5. spec §4** | The vocabulary paragraph now says **`pat=` is a literal string**, matched byte for byte (its UTF-8 bytes), every character standing for itself, one hit per line as `grep -rnF` counts. The example body is no longer `grep -rn SABOTAGE src/` but a sentence (*"the literal text SABOTAGE on no line of any file under src/ …"*), and the body is said to be prose for the reader. **§6** gains one bullet: deliberate disguise (NBSP, ZWSP, HTML with attributes, table, link, task list) is out of scope, per D18. The checker's own "WHAT THIS CHECKER CANNOT SEE" header says the same. |

**New suite rows** (all MEASURED green on the final bytes, and red on S1-fix5's checker and on
the sabotages below):
- **Q9**: a plain fixture whose `enc/` holds `caf\xe9/note.txt` (2 lines), `\xff\xfe.txt` (1),
  `café-utf8/n.txt` (1) and `plain.txt` (1), made with the suite's **own** switch to iso8859-1 so
  the fixture does not depend on the code under test. Under the system encoding forced to utf-8
  **and** to iso8859-1 (the checker's root re-read as that locale would have decoded it): the
  whole of `enc` counts **5** and `path=enc/café-utf8` counts **1**. Then the fallback: the
  scan's inner `scan_dir`, called **without** the byte scope under utf-8 (the S1-fix5 condition,
  rebuilt on purpose), must name the entry. Then the gate: a stamped file declaring ENCTOKEN
  absent from `enc` is RED with 5 hits. Want `{5 1 5 1 named 1}`.
- **Q10**: nine files. A FALSE `assert=absent pat=SABOTAGE path=src state=holds` in a `~~~`
  fence, an indented fence, a blockquote, a list item (`1. ```sh …`), a never-closed fence, a fence
  inside a 4-backtick block, an `ASSERT=` key, and a grandfathered file: each exactly one named
  problem naming its cause. The known negative (a READ assert= that holds, and `assert=` in prose)
  gives none. Text-only, so it runs in every state.
- **Q11**: in process, a link out of the corpus (to a file whose stamp names `deadbee0`), a link
  to a sibling, a dangling link and a regular file (the negative): `{1 1 0 1}`, and **no problem
  mentions deadbee0** (nothing read it); a baseline that is a link: `BASELINE UNUSABLE`; a plain
  checkout whose `doc/claude/issues` is a link out: exactly one `CORPUS OUTSIDE THE CHECKOUT`.
  The FIFO half (a FIFO issue file, a link to a FIFO, a FIFO baseline) runs in a **child `tclsh`
  under `timeout 60`**, so a regressed checker makes the row red by name (*"TIMED OUT: the gate
  blocked on a FIFO"*) instead of hanging the suite that checks it. Want `{1 1 0 1 0 1 1 {1 1 1}}`.
- **Q12**: three grandfathered files, three lines each of 50k `>` + a stamp, 20k `- ` + a stamp,
  and 50k `>` + the bare word `stamp`: `{3 3 0}` named problems and the whole gate in **under 2 s**.
- **The suite's own corpus reads** (`istamp_read_issue`, used by B1, B4 and the D9 census) now
  lstat first, with the suite's own lstat rather than the checker's, so a planted FIFO names
  itself there too instead of blocking B1.

### Contract A, CPU: every parser path is linear (MEASURED, `stress.tcl`)

Two files per shape (one stamped, one grandfathered), each carrying one line of N characters,
gated. N = 100k → 1M, ms: `>`-run + stamp 3 → 18; `- `-run 3 → 17; a backtick run carrying
quote=/assert= 6 → 46; a backtick line inside a quote block 2 → 20; `*`-runs around STAMP: 4 → 43;
`_`-runs 4 → 41; `<`-runs 6 → 45; repeated `<strong>` 45 → 369; `quote= assert=` tokens on one
fence 25 → 195; repeated `stamp ` 2 → 17; 5k → 50k blockquoted stamp lines 30 → 249; 2k → 22k
assert fences 22 → 203. **Ten times the input, about ten times the time, every shape.**

### Every exec and every open in the final bytes (READ, `grep -n` of both files)

The checker's exec lines are **unchanged from S1-fix5**: the one git exec in `git_env` (and its
`2>@1` words re-run), and `report`'s literal-only `grep -rnoE`. Its opens: `read_file` (issue
files and the baseline, now reached **only** after lstat says regular file; absolute paths whose
names match `^[0-9]{4}-`), and `scan_file` (absolute paths from the walk, inside `in_bytes`). No
word from an issue file reaches any argv except a `rev_token`-checked revision behind
`--end-of-options`, as before. The suite gained two execs, both on this run's scratch paths only:
Q11's `exec timeout 10 mkfifo -- <3 scratch paths>` and `exec timeout 60 tclsh <scratch>/drv.tcl`.
The suite changes `encoding system` in three places (Q9's fixture, `q9_eval`, Q9's fallback
column), each restored on every path.

### Deviations

1. **`istamp_file_url` (the suite) now takes a path's bytes through the system encoding, not
   UTF-8.** Not in D18's list. Found while measuring a new stranger shape, a clone under a UTF-8
   `café/` directory: under LANG=C (Tcl's system encoding iso8859-1) the H-fixture build
   double-encoded the path, git answered `'…/cafÃ©/…' does not appear to be a git repository`, and
   **19 rows went red for a tree that is fine**: `19 FAILED (64 passed)` on S1-fix5's bytes, the
   same set on the intermediate bytes (MEASURED). The fix is one token in a file this stage owns;
   sabotage U1 holds it. Under C.UTF-8 nothing changes.
2. **The corpus directory is confined too** (`issues_dir_problem`). D18.3 names the issue files and
   the baseline; a committed link at `doc/claude/issues` (or `doc`, `doc/claude`) is the same read
   outside the checkout one level up (contract A). It is asked only of the checkout's own corpus,
   never of a corpus handed to `gate <dir> <baseline>` or to `with_corpus`, and a link that stays
   inside is followed (MEASURED `ok` in `rf6b`). Q11 holds it; R4 and C2 redden it.
3. **A link that points inside the checkout is refused too.** D18 says "a symlink … is a named
   problem"; the rule is about what the entry IS, so no target can argue with it (git tracks issue
   files as files). MEASURED impact on the real corpus: 0 (no issue file is a link).
4. **An issue file whose NAME is not UTF-8** is named, but differently by locale: under C.UTF-8,
   `issue_files`' glob name does not round-trip and lstat says *"cannot be looked at"*; under LANG=C
   it is read. Both are red and named; the byte scope was applied to the assert= scan (item 1), not
   to the corpus listing. Making the listing byte-exact too would change the checker's text
   handling of every issue file, and fail-closed already holds.
5. **The unstamped-file wording** is *"an assert= the parser does not read (in a file with no
   **STAMP:** line, where no assert= is ever evaluated)"*: the task's *"assert= in a file with no
   stamp is not evaluated"*, in the same shape as quote='s existing sentence.
6. **`report`** also stops opening non-regular files and states the baseline's condition. It is
   advisory (exit 0) and runs the same lstat rule, so the CLI cannot hang in either mode.
7. **Q9 emulates the two locales in process** (it forces `encoding system`), because a suite runs
   under one locale. The real-locale evidence is separate: the main tree and `café/xschem` under
   `LANG=C LC_ALL=C` on tclsh, T1 and CLI, and every item-1 recipe in both.
8. **A tool slip, found and fixed before freezing:** the edit tool wrote my `é` escape into
   Q9's code as a literal UTF-8 `é`, which `source` reads as two characters under LANG=C; Q9 went red
   there (MEASURED). It is now the ASCII escape, and a byte grep shows non-ASCII only on comment
   lines in both files (as before this stage).
9. **Three concurrency slips, all re-measured:**
   - the two locales once ran concurrently in the same `café` clone, so one run's end count saw
     the other's live directories as "26 corpses". The solo S1-fix5 run there was 0→0;
   - `misc.sh` and `defects.sh` both use the `xschem` clone and first ran concurrently. Both were
     re-run **solo**, misc in `xschem` and defects in `sab3`, with identical verdicts; those solo
     logs are what this receipt quotes and scores. The one remaining `0→1` (`def_quote`) is misc's
     read-only-checkout run's `/tmp/istamp_*_tmproot` (14:19:49-14:20:31) seen by `run.sh`'s global
     count at 14:19:51; afterwards every `.scratch` and `/tmp/istamp_*` was empty;
   - the first main-tree `diff_assert` ran while the main-tree battery wrote `.scratch` under
     `tests/`, and its reference grep got `é` through Tcl's iso8859-1 exec encoding. It was re-run
     with the main tree quiet and the reference passing UTF-8 bytes.
10. **The xschem and T1 arms use the main tree's `src/xschem`** (built 09-17), as every earlier
    round did. The shapes were not built.

### Open (not fixed here)

- **Deliberate disguise is out of scope** (D18), now stated in the spec §6 and the checker header:
  NBSP/ZWSP prefixes, HTML with attributes, table cells, links, task lists, and a second stamp
  hidden that way still pass (the e2e plant's NBSP stamp, MEASURED).
- **lstat-then-open is not atomic.** A file swapped for a FIFO between the two calls would block
  `open`. That needs a concurrent writer in the checkout, not a corpus shape (INFERRED); Tcl 8.6's
  `open` has no `O_NOFOLLOW`.
- **A row that throws outside its own `catch` still kills the suite** in the plain `check` rows
  that build fixtures inline: Q5 as before, and the in-process half of Q11 (`file rename`/`file
  link` after the guarded fixture block). None of those calls failed in any shape measured
  (READ; theoretical).
- **Q11 needs `tclsh` and `mkfifo` on PATH**, as S20 needs `tclsh`. With either missing, Q11 is red
  by name (`DRIVER DIED` / `FIFO FIXTURE NOT BUILT`), never hung (READ).
- **A checkout under a directory that is NOT valid UTF-8, under C.UTF-8,** was not measured.
  Tcl's own `info script` path would not round-trip there, so the checker could not read its own
  tree (INFERRED; pre-existing).
- **The suite's `istamp_listing`** (Q5's "was anything written" evidence) still skips a name lstat
  cannot see. It is evidence-only and blind in the direction of a missed write under a non-UTF-8
  name (READ).
- **Carried over:** `summarize_all` drops `skip:` lines (D12); `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is unmeasured;
  `HAVE_GIT` depends on `timeout`; `quote_holds` in an online blob:none clone may still lazily
  fetch the quoted blob (latent: 0 quote blocks in the corpus); uutils `timeout` costs 100 ms per
  exec; the redundant layer I2.

### Real home

- `md5sum -c --quiet …/xschem_manifest_fixes.md5` gave rc 0 before any work and rc 0 after all of
  it.

## S1-fix7

**Crew** S1-fix7. **Date** 2026-09-18. **Tree** `fluid-editing` at `aa5cece0` (the driver's commit of
S1-fix6). The checker, the suite, the baseline and the spec were edited in place in the main tree
and are not committed; the driver holds git. Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix7/`. Shapes are
in `shapes/`, all cloned from a scratch mirror of the main tree (`shapes/mirror.git`, with
`uploadpack.allowFilter`); per-run logs are in `logs/runs/<tag>.<arm>.log`. Scripts: `rf7.sh`
(D19's recipes, the S1-fix6 refuter's plus this stage's extras), `cpu.sh` (the 100-block recipe),
`diff_parse7.tcl`, `latin.sh`, `sab.py`/`sablane.sh`, `arms.sh`, `battery_main.sh`, and
`rf6.sh`, `rf6b.sh`, `textplants.sh`, `misc.sh`, `hermetic.sh`, `defects_sab3.sh`, `score.sh`
ported from S1-fix6. aa5cece0's bytes (`81b5885c` / `865d94a3`, baseline `2a14c0e3`, spec
`03881750`) are kept in `files/pre/`, and every "aa5cece0 bytes" column below ran on them. Tags:
**MEASURED**, **READ**, **INFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**.

**Final md5** (frozen in `files/final/`; the main tree was md5-checked equal at the end):

- `tests/headless/test_issue_stamp.tcl c4741eaa426db25049623a0871e3cc4b`
