### What changed, by D19 item

| D19 item | change |
|---|---|
| **A1. every reader honours the corpus confinement; report runs nothing** | `report` is now `report_census` (a dict) plus a printer. It asks `issues_dir_problem` **before** listing anything, lstats each issue file (`not_regular`) before opening it, and counts citations with a Tcl walk, `cite_count` / `cite_dir`. That walk resolves `doc/claude/issues`, `src` and `tests` from the checkout's real root through `resolve_in`, the same as an `assert=` `path=`; a directory that leads out is `not counted: … leads out of the checkout`, by name. It follows no link below the top, opens only regular files, skips a file holding a NUL (grep prints no line for one), reads names in bytes (`in_bytes`), and runs under one clock (`t_scan_total`). **The grep exec is gone, so the checker's only exec is git.** In the suite, B1, B4 and the D9 census list the corpus only through `istamp_corpus_files`, which asks the checker's `issues_dir_problem` first; when it answers, they read nothing and are red **by name** (`NOT-READ <the CORPUS sentence>`, and H9 carries the same). They are procs so that row **Q13** can drive them. |
| **A2. `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` on every git call** | `git_env` merges `git_always {GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH 1}` into every call **after** the call site's own variables, so no call site can drop it, and it is saved and restored like the rest. **Consequence, handled:** with lazy fetch off, a blob:none clone answers `<rev>:<path> missing` for an old revision's file that it simply never fetched. That is the same answer as for a path the revision never had, so a quote in a stranger's partial clone would have turned RED. `quote_holds` now asks `quote_gap` about a `missing`, only for a revision the gate has **verified**. The gap needs three pieces of evidence: the repository's own config names a promisor remote (`config --local`); `ls-tree -r` of the revision, which reads trees only, lists the path as a blob (the path is matched in Tcl, never put on argv); and that blob is not in the store. When all three hold, the quote is **NOT VERIFIED by name**, `(partial clone: <rev> has <path>, and its content is not in this checkout -- this checker never fetches)`. A path the revision lacks, a rotted quote whose blob is present, an unresolved quote revision and the same missing blob in a store that is not a partial clone all stay RED. The CLI tail says `full: content a partial clone never fetched`, not `history absent`. D9h's empty-skip column leaves such a gap out, and it is still printed by name. |
| **A3. a total budget for `assert=` scans** | `t_scan_total` (60 s). `gate` is now a wrapper that sets a run-wide `scan_deadline` and clears it on every way out; the body is `gate_body`. `scan_budget` gives each scan the earlier of its own 60 s and the run's deadline, with the matching sentence. `assert_eval` checks the run's clock **before** reading anything, so a block reached after it has run out is named, and not one file is read for it. |
| **B1. a read fence's info string is parsed in full** | New `fence_info`. A fence is *marked* when any word of its info string is shaped `key=value`. A marked fence may then carry at most one leading language word and after it only `key=value` words, each key in `fence_keys` (`quote path fix assert pat state`) and each given once. Every other word goes into the block's `bad` list, and the gate names the block (`a marked fence the parser cannot read in full -- <word> is not a key=value word …`) and **does not evaluate it**. A fence with no key=value word is an ordinary code sample, as before (row N2). |
| **B2. a line carrying a stamp body is named** | `stray_stamps` also names any line holding `` `v1 claim= `` (whitespace-tolerant) that `find_stamp` does not read and that the stamp-shape rule did not already name: `a line carrying a stamp's body (…) that is not a **STAMP:** line the parser reads (it is preceded by `**STAMP**`)`. |
| **B3. grandfathering by exact file name; misnamed files named** | `issue_stamp_baseline.txt` lists **1037 file names** where it listed 1037 numbers. Each number named exactly one unstamped file (MEASURED), so the **grandfathered set of files is unchanged**, and `diff_parse7.tcl` confirms it file by file (below). `load_baseline` keys by name, and refuses the whole baseline, by line, when a line is a bare number (`has a bare issue number on line N`) or anything else that is not a comment or a name. `canonical_name` is the one name rule (`^[0-9]{4}-.*\.md$`), and `name_key` reads a listed name as UTF-8 in every locale. `misnamed_files` names every entry that begins with a digit and ends in `.md` in any case but is not canonical. It opens nothing. `report` counts them. |
| **spec** | §4: the info-string rule, and the per-run `assert=` budget. §5: grandfathering by exact file name, misnamed names, stamp-body lines, why the baseline lists names and not numbers, and a new paragraph "What the checker runs, and what it reads" (git only; every reader asks `issues_dir_problem`; `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`; the partial-clone quote). |
| **Limit (spec §6)** | A checkout whose own path is not valid UTF-8 is unsupported. The bullet gives both mechanisms: under C.UTF-8 the path does not round-trip through Tcl, and under LANG=C uutils `timeout` 0.8.0 refuses non-UTF-8 argv. The checker header says the same in one sentence. §6 also gains the partial-clone quote as NOT VERIFIED. |

  `status_annotate.md` and 9 numbered attachments (`0054-*.tcl`, `0054-*.c`, six `*.patch`). No
  number is shared by two canonical files. **No entry begins with a digit, ends in `.md` in any
  case and misses the canonical name, so B3's name rule names no real file.** Nothing had to be
  hidden, so there was nothing to report.
- **Baseline.** 1037 numbers. Each names exactly one file, none of those files is stamped, and
  the 10 stamped files (0056 0216 0249 0442 0650 0891 0905 1219 1438 1458) are the only canonical
  files outside it.

  Four lines hold a backticked `v1` for other reasons, such as `` `v1 v2 v3` `` and
  `` `v1 = 8.333333e-01` ``, and they are the self-test's known negatives.
- **Marked fences.** One column-0 backtick fence in the corpus carries an `=`: 1219:72, `sh
  assert=absent pat=SABOTAGE path=src state=broken`, fully in the grammar.

### Every exec and every open in the final bytes, with provenance (READ, `grep -n` of both files)

**Checker.**
- **Exactly two exec lines, both git, both in `git_env`:** `exec timeout $t_git git -C $dir
  {*}$args 2>/dev/null`, and its failure-words re-run `… 2>@1`. `report`'s `/usr/bin/grep` exec
  is **gone**.
- `$dir` is the checker's own repo or `init <dir>`, never corpus text. Every call now runs with
  `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`.
- Call sites carrying corpus-derived words:
  - `rev_exists`, `rev_type`, `rev_is_ancestor`: a `rev_token`-checked revision behind
    `--end-of-options`;
  - `quote_holds`: `<rev>:<path>` on **stdin** only;
  - `quote_gap` (new): `config --local --get-regexp <literal>`, `ls-tree -r -z --full-tree
    --end-of-options <rev>` (the hex revision; the path is compared in Tcl, never passed), and
    `cat-file -e --end-of-options <oid>` (a hex name git itself printed).
- All other call sites are literals.
- **Opens:**
  - `read_file`: issue files, only after `not_regular`; and the baseline;
  - `scan_file`: absolute paths from the confined walk;
  - `cite_dir` (new): absolute paths from the confined walk, regular files only.

**Suite execs.**
- `HAVE_GIT` (`git --version`); S20's driver (`tclsh <scratch>/drv.tcl`); the fixture `sh -c`
  (scratch paths and file:// URLs); H0 (`rev-parse --local-env-vars`); H15 (`git -C <fixture>
  rev-parse HEAD`); Q11's `mkfifo` and child `tclsh` (scratch paths).
- **H22 (new):** `env GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1 timeout 30 git -C <fixture> cat-file -e <hex>`. The hex
  is the fixture script's own `rev-parse` output.
- Git calls through the checker's `git_in`: `istamp_test_rev`, and Q1's `show`.

**No word from an issue file reaches any argv except a `rev_token`-checked revision behind
`--end-of-options`.**

X1 and I3 were run **because** Q5's and H17's fixtures changed (deviation 8). They prove that the
relative, whitespace-free targets still catch the regression each row exists for.

### Deviations

1. **The partial-clone quote gap (`quote_gap`) was not in D19's list.** A2 made it necessary. With
   lazy fetch off, a genuine quote of an old revision in a blob:none clone reads `missing`, and
   without the gap it would have been a false red in a shape that must be green. It also closes the
   limit every round carried: "an offline blob:none partial clone scores an older `quote=` as rot".
   It is fail-closed in three ways: only a verified revision, only in a repository whose own
   config names a promisor, and only when the revision's trees list the path. H22 holds each of
   these (N2, N3b, N4). Its CLI tail and D9h's exclusion follow from it.
2. **`report` confines and Tcl-scans `src` and `tests` too**, not only the issues directory D19
   names. MEASURED red-first: a `src` link out made the census count 7000 lines outside the
   checkout. Removing the grep exec entirely is the item's own logic, one step further.
3. **B1 also names an unknown key and a key given twice**, not only a word that is not key=value.
   Both were silently tolerated (a duplicate `pat=` took the second value); "fully parsed" was read
   as the stamp grammar's rule. A fence with **no** key=value word is left alone, so ordinary code
   fences with titles are not over-fired on (0 real fences are affected, MEASURED).
4. **The baseline refuses a line it cannot read, as a whole.** A bare number or a stray line makes
   `BASELINE UNUSABLE`, by line number, rather than being skipped. A skipped line would
   un-grandfather a file without a word. A stale number-format baseline beside the new checker is
   therefore loud (MEASURED incidentally, deviation 11).
5. **`gate` became a wrapper around `gate_body`**, so the run's deadline is cleared on every way
   out. No caller changed.
6. **One self-test known negative was re-expected, not dropped.** `# see **STAMP:** <a whole
   body> in the spec` was a known negative. Under B2 it is exactly what D19 names, so that spelling
   became a positive, and the mention negative now reads `` `v1 ...` `` (as Q4's 9015 always has).
7. **B1 lists uncovered file names, not numbers**, and **D9h** leaves partial-clone content gaps
   out of its empty-skip column (they are printed by name).
8. **Q5 and H17's fixtures now build whitespace-free, relative targets.** The first final battery
   (suite `a8b1b885`) measured both red on three arms in `we ird[x] %41/xschem`. Their `pat=>…` and
   `quote=--output=…` values were built from the scratch path, which holds a space there. B1 now
   correctly names that as a two-word info string, which made a stranger's clean tree red. Q5 now
   gates from inside its fixture checkout with `pat=>../out/pwned`. H17 uses
   `--output=../../istamp_<pid>_pwned/gate`, relative to the fixture repository git runs in.
   Sabotages X1 and I3 re-prove both. ALL PASS 93 in that shape afterwards.
9. **The run harness, not the checker, broke the emoji shape's xschem and T1 arms on the first
   pass.** My tag put the emoji into HOME, and the xschem binary answered `Tcl_AppInit(): failure
   creating …/.xschem`. The arms were re-run with ASCII tags: ALL PASS 93. The binary's behaviour
   under such a HOME is outside these files and is recorded, not investigated (UNKNOWN cause).
10. **The xschem and T1 arms use the main tree's `src/xschem`**, as every earlier round did. The
    shapes were not built.
11. **Slips, found and re-measured:**
    - `rf7.sh`'s restore first re-checked-out the committed baseline into the clone under test.
      Every final-bytes recipe in that pass hit `BASELINE UNUSABLE`. Fixed, and re-run;
    - `textplants.sh` first ran without its markers directory, so its "nothing written" column
      proved nothing. Re-run with it present: 0 markers;
    - `score.sh` was first given relative paths after its `cd`. Re-run;
    - an intermediate sabotage pass ran on suite `fe43ed2c` and checker `5d632471`, before two
      wording edits. The table above is the re-run on the frozen bytes.

### Open (not fixed here)

- **Redundant layer (N3).** In `quote_gap`, the promisor-value test is held by no row alone,
  because `git config --get-regexp` already exits 1 when no promisor key exists. It matters only for
  a configured `remote.<r>.promisor=false`. The pair is held (N3b).
- **Branches no row exercises** (READ, fail-closed or advisory):
  - a partial clone that lacks the revision's **tree** (a `tree:0` filter), where the quote is
    NOT VERIFIED `the tree of <rev> is not in this checkout either`;
  - `cite_dir` silently leaving out a directory it cannot list (report is advisory).
- **report's binary rule is NUL only.** grep also treats a file with an encoding error as binary
  under a UTF-8 locale. Counts measured equal to grep on this tree in both locales anyway.
- **An older git** (before 2.44) ignores `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` and would fetch (INFERRED from git's
  release notes; this box has 2.53).
- **Carried over:**
  - lstat-then-open is not atomic;
  - the non-UTF-8 checkout path (now recorded in spec §6);
  - `summarize_all` drops `skip:` lines (D12);
  - `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is unmeasured;
  - `HAVE_GIT` depends on `timeout`;
  - uutils `timeout` costs ~100 ms per exec;
  - redundant layer I2.
- **The spec's §5 sentence "the suite carries 34 checks of which 8 are red-observed"** is a stale
  count (93 today). It was left as prose, per §3 rule 3.

prescribed ````. The checker and spec did not change (`accdf887` / `b373429b` throughout). Everything

their runs on checker `accdf887`, which the re-freeze did not touch.

### What changed, by item

| item | change |
|---|---|
| **1. A fence is marked only by a known key** | `fence_info` marks a fence only when a word is `quote=…` or `assert=…` (`fence_marks`), the two keys the gate evaluates. Any other fence is an ordinary code sample and its info string is not read: ```` ```python title="example.py" ````, ```` ```sh cc=gcc make ````, `prefix=/usr`, `hl_lines`/`linenums`, and a `fix=` label. Inside a marked fence nothing changed: every word must be a known `key=value`, each key once, after at most one language word. So the multi-word `pat=`, an unknown key, a repeated key, a stray word after `quote=`, and a `title=` in a marked fence are still named. A near-miss of the two keys (`ASSERT=`, `assert =`) now falls through to `stray_attrs`, which names it once (S1-fix7 named it twice). |
| **2. A stamp body inside a closed fence is an example** | `fence_scan` also returns `inside`, the body-line ranges of every fence that closes (backtick or tilde, indented up to 3). `stray_stamps` skips the body check (`` `v1 claim= ``) only on those lines. A body in prose, after a fence has closed, in a fence the file never closes, or on a fence's own opening line is still named. The colon-less typos (Q16) are unchanged. The stamp-SHAPED check (`STAMP:` plus a body) was not touched (open item 1). |
| **3. Each budget is charged with what it names** | The `assert=` total (`t_scan_total`, 60 s) is now **scanning time**: `assert_eval` times its own walk and scan and adds it to `scan_spent_us` on every way out (`scan_charge`). Git work, parsing and everything else are not charged to it. The wall clock is kept as a **separate, named budget** for the whole gate run, `t_gate_total` (600 s, under T1's 900 s per-case cap). Once it is spent, each `tree=` (per file), `quote=` and `assert=` the gate reaches is a named problem saying that the gate's time ran out (`gate_spent`, `gate_msg`), never passed. `scan_budget` gives a scan the earliest of its own 60 s, the scanning time left, and the gate's deadline, each with the sentence that names it. `gate` sets and clears both. |
| **4. No problem line is unbounded** | `clip` quotes a corpus word whole up to N characters, else as its first N and `...(<len> characters)`. It is applied to every stamp token `parse_stamp` quotes, and to `pat=`, `path=`, `state=`, `assert=` kind, quote paths, the partial-clone sentences and `confine_path`'s resolve error. `fence_info` names the first 5 stray words and counts the rest (`and N more word(s) like these, not listed`), so it never builds a 200k-item list. `gate` passes every problem and every `NOT VERIFIED` line through `cap_lines`, a 2000-character backstop that says it cut. |
| **spec** | §4: the marking rule, and both budgets. §5: the fenced-body exception, the near-miss list and the problem-line bound. §6: two new limits (a stamp written in a fence is still read as one; the 600 s wall clock depends on the box). |

### New rows (MEASURED green on the final bytes; red on the base checker and on the sabotages below)

- **Q18** (no git, plain scratch root): one gate over eight stamped files. Five must be green: an mkdocs
  title, `cc=gcc make`, `prefix=/usr install`, a `fix=superseded` label followed by a comment, and
  `hl_lines`/`linenums`. Three must be named once each: a `title=` in a marked fence, a multi-word `pat=`,
  and `ASSERT=`. Want `{0 0 0 0 0 1 1 1 3}`.
- **Q19** (no git): a body in a ```` ``` ```` fence, in a `~~~` fence (colon-less) and in a four-backtick
  fence, in grandfathered files; the first again in a stamped file. All must be green. Named once each: a
  mid-sentence body, a body after a fence closes, a body in a fence never closed, a body on a fence's
  opening line, and a colon-less second stamp under a real one. Want `{0 0 0 1 1 1 1 0 1}`.
- **Q20** (no git): time is made deterministic by `trace add execution … enter {q20_sleep N}`. Nothing in
  the checker changes.
  - **A:** 300 ms of non-scan work per file (in `parse_stamp`), four files, 0.4 s of scanning allowed. Want
    0 problems.
  - **B:** 300 ms of scanning per block (in `assert_scan`), the same allowance. The scanning budget must
    name 2 or 3 blocks, and every problem must name it. Want `1 0` (in range, and none naming the gate).
  - **C:** `t_gate_total` 0.5 s, spent inside the first `tree=` question (900 ms). The quote, the assertion
    after it, and the next file's `tree=` must each be named as not checked because the gate's time ran
    out. Want `{1 1 1} 3`. Then both budgets must be cleared.
  - Want overall `{0 1 0 {1 1 1} 3 1}`. Row time is about 3 s.
- **Q21** (no git): a marked fence of 20 000 stray words, a 100 000-character `pat=` and `path=`, and a
  `quote=` whose `path=` is 100 000 characters (a `NOT VERIFIED` line in the plain root). Each must carry
  its count or clip marker, and every line must be under 1000 characters. Want `{1 0 1 0 1 0 1 0 3}`.
- **Q14** is re-worded for the scanning budget. Its clear-check reads `scan_spent_us` and `gate_deadline`,
  and is false (never an error) when they do not exist.

### Deviations

1. **Item 3 kept the wall clock under its own name, as a second budget.** The item asks for "a bound on
   total work, with a truthful name for whatever is exhausted". The scanning-time budget alone bounds
   assert= work: it is still 60 s, and A3 is still 61.6 s. But the old wall clock was the only thing
   that ever stopped a gate whose git work ran long, and the S1-fix7 refuter measured that git work as
   unbounded: 50 bogus-revision quotes took 16 s, and a partial clone runs an uncached `ls-tree -r` per
   absent quote. So `t_gate_total` (600 s) bounds the whole run. It stops `tree=`, `quote=` and
   `assert=` alike, by name, fail-closed. It sits under T1's 900 s per-case cap, and it is about a
   thousand times the real CLI gate (0.6 s, self-test included). The cost: on a box slow enough that the real gate takes 600 s, the gate is
   red and says why (spec §6).
2. **A `fix=`-only fence is no longer a block.** The task names the marking keys as "quote= or assert=".
   Nothing ever evaluated `fix=`: `gate_body` reads `quote` and `assert` only (READ). So no verdict can
   change, and `find_blocks` no longer returns a `fix=`-only block, which no caller reads. The spec §4
   `fix=` example is unchanged; its info string is simply not read now.
3. **The body skip covers only fences that close.** A body in a fence the file never closes, or on a
   fence's own opening line, is still named: fail-closed, because markdown's reading of either depends on
   what follows. Q19 holds both, and sabotages G2 and G3 prove it.
4. **Item 4 goes beyond "first N stray words".** Every corpus word quoted in a problem is clipped at its
   site, and there is a 2000-character backstop on every problem and `NOT VERIFIED` line. The recipes
   found 1–3 MB lines from `pat=`, `path=`, a quote path and a stamp token, not only from stray words.
5. **Q14's clear-check tolerates a missing variable** (it is false, not an error), so that on the base
   checker Q14 and Q20 report every part instead of `ROW DIED`.
6. **The suite was re-frozen once** (F2, above). The first sabotage pass and the first arm battery (suite
   `059deffc`, `logs/arms_preview/`, `logs/sab_preview/`) were otherwise identical in every verdict.
7. **Harness slip, re-run.** My path rewrite of the refuter's `rf6.sh` pointed its `restore` at a
   nonexistent `rv/files/final`. Its first recipe deleted `plant`'s baseline, so rf6, rf6b, rf7 and
   reportplants on that first pass all ran against `BASELINE MISSING` and are void. Fixed with
   `rv/files` → `files`, and the whole class-A set was re-run on the frozen bytes (the table above).
   None of this touched the owned files.
8. As in every earlier round, the xschem and T1 arms use the main tree's `src/xschem`. The shapes were not
   built.

### Open (not fixed here)

1. **A stamp written in a fence is still read as one.** A canonical `**STAMP:**` line at column 0 inside a
   fence is read by `find_stamp`: `2 STAMP lines` in a stamped file, `the STAMP is at line 95` in
   grandfathered 0057. `STAMP:` plus a body inside a fence is named by the stamp-shaped check. Both
   MEASURED, identical at aa5cece0, on the base bytes and on the final bytes (fail-closed reds), and the
   real corpus has neither. Item 2 was scoped to the body check. Spec §6 records this.
2. **A fence inside a blockquote or list item is not a fence** to `fence_scan` (carried, §4), so a body
   quoted there is still named.
3. **The gate's 600 s wall clock depends on the box** (spec §6). Quote work is bounded by it now, but it is
   not faster: `ls-tree` is still not cached per revision (the refuter's efficiency note).
4. **Q20 uses real sleeps.** Part C needs the gate to reach its first `tree=` within 0.5 s. That setup
   takes milliseconds. Q20 was green in 56 logs on the frozen suite, under concurrent load from up to seven
   suites at once (MEASURED). So the margin is wide, but it is a margin.
5. **K2 (the 2000-character backstop disabled) is held by D9b alone.** Every known site now clips first,
   so no gate-level input reaches the backstop. The self-test holds it by known answer.
6. **Carried from S1-fix7:** lstat-then-open is not atomic; the non-UTF-8 checkout path (spec §6);
   `summarize_all` drops `skip:` lines (D12); `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` is unmeasured; `HAVE_GIT` depends on
   `timeout`; uutils `timeout` costs ~100 ms per exec; redundant layers I2 and N3; the `tree:0` branch is
   held by no row; git before 2.44 ignores `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` (INFERRED); the stale "34 checks" in
   spec §5.

### What changed

| item | change |
|---|---|
| **R2: `fence_marks`** | The refuter's remedy, verbatim: `{quote assert path pat state}`. A fence whose marking key is misspelled still carries `path=`, `pat=` or `state=`, so it is read, and `asert=` / `assertion=` / `asserts=` / `qoute=` / `quotes=` is named as a key the grammar does not know. |
| **ASSERT=/QUOTE= named once** (the task's extra) | `fence_info` now **leaves** a near-miss of `quote=`/`assert=` to `stray_attrs`: a word matching `^(quote\|assert)=` in another case, and the spaced forms `assert =`, `assert =absent` and `assert = absent`. It reads the rest of the info string without those words. It does so only when (a) `stray_attrs`' own test, `(^\|[ \t])k[ \t]*=` with `-nocase`, matches the same info string, and (b) the key is **not** also written canonically in it. When it is, `stray_attrs` is silent for that line (`used`), so the variant stays a bad word here and is still named once. The self-test answer for `{sh ASSERT=absent pat=x path=src state=holds}` is `{3 0}`, not the remedy's `{3 1}`. |
| **R3: `fence_scan` opener** | The refuter's remedy, verbatim: a backtick fence whose info string holds a backtick does not open (CommonMark). |
| **R3: `stray_attrs` cause** (beyond the remedy) | A ```` ``` ```` line whose info string holds a backtick and carries `quote=`/`assert=` is named with its real cause: `a ``` line whose info string holds a backtick, which markdown reads as inline code, not a fence`. With the remedy alone it fell through to `inside another fenced block`. |
| **R1: `stray_stamps`' `inside`** | The refuter's remedy, verbatim: `fence_scan [regsub -all -line {^(?:[ \t]*>)*[ \t]*} $text ""]`. It is used only for the body-skip ranges. The block reader, `find_blocks`, still scans the raw text. |
| **spec** | §4 covers the five marking keys and why, the once-naming, and a new paragraph **"Which lines are fences"** (the CommonMark opener rule). §5 covers the body exception through containers, `<pre>` and indented code, the ```` ```inline``` ```` line, and the near-miss list with its once-rule. §6 **replaces** the wrong sentence ("a fence inside a blockquote or a list item is not seen as a fence") with **"Which fences are seen, exactly"**, which has three sub-bullets: the block reader, the stamp-body exception (container-stripped) with its two mis-pairing shapes, and a list-item marker on the fence's own line. §6 also gains **"A block that lost its marking key entirely is not named"**, which states both costs of the path/pat/state marking. |

**Spec §6 claims, each measured** (`scripts/spec_claims.tcl`, `logs/spec_claims.out`, final against
S1-fix8, `stray_stamps` counts):

| case | S1-fix8 | final | what it shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| a list-item fence at 3 spaces | 0 | 0 | seen by both readings |
| blockquoted fence | 1 | 0 | R1 |
| fence under a nested list, 5 spaces | 1 | 0 | R1 |
| `> > ` fence | 1 | 0 | R1 |
| tab-indented fence | 1 | 0 | R1 |
| an indented code block holding ```` ``` ```` lines | 1 | 0 | code either way |
| **a blockquoted fence whose body line lost its `>`** | 1 | **0** | the stated mis-pairing limit |
| **```` ``` ```` indented 4 directly under a paragraph** | 1 | **0** | the stated mis-pairing limit |
| `- ```text` (list marker on the fence line) | 1 | 1 | named, as the spec says |

The block reader, on both versions: a list-item `assert=` fence at 3 spaces is **opened** (`opened={3 1}`)
and named `a fence that is indented`. The 5-space and blockquoted ones are not opened and are named
`indented` / `inside a blockquote`.

### Deviations

1. **The handoff to `stray_attrs` goes beyond the remedy, as the task required.** The refuter's R2
   remedy alone names `ASSERT=`, `QUOTE=` and `assert =` twice. Q18 goes red, which the refuter predicted,
   and Q23 goes red too (measured on its bytes). The handoff is gated on `stray_attrs`' own regex and on
   the key's absence, so it cannot leave a word to a check that will stay silent. S9c2 proves that guard.
2. **A new `stray_attrs` cause for a ```` ``` ```` line with a backtick in its info string.** Without it,
   the remedy names such a line `inside another fenced block`, which is false.
   **Behavioural change against S1-fix8:** ```` ```sh assert=absent pat=`static` path=… ```` used to be
   read as a block, with a pattern holding backticks. It is now one named problem, because markdown shows
   no code block there (Q24's 9256). d42fc517 read it too.
3. **Q23 carries `QUOTE=` and `assert =` beside the misspellings**, so the once-rule is held at gate level.
   Q10's per-cause needle could not see a second problem.
4. **The main tree's HEAD moved `334dc0d5` → `7a46275f` during the run** (another crew's HOME work, which

   built.

## S1-fix9 — refutation (adversarial verifier, 2026-09-18)

**Verdict: REFUTED. There are two regression families, both MEASURED and both introduced by the R1 remedy. There is no class-A finding.**
The cause is one line. `stray_stamps` now runs `fence_scan` over `regsub -all -line {^(?:[ \t]*>)*[ \t]*} $text ""`, and it
applies that strip to the whole text, including the bodies of real column-0 fences. So a `> ```` or `    ```` line *inside* a
```` ```md ````, ```` ```python ```` or similar fence becomes a closer. The real closer then opens a phantom fence. That phantom
runs to the next bare ```` ``` ```` of some later fence, and it inverts the body check's view of everything in between. A lone
```` ``` ```` line in an indented code block, or in a blockquoted fence that the quote leaves unclosed, pairs with a later fence
in the same way. None of these shapes is a disguise: all are valid CommonMark.
Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix9_verify/`. There is a mirror of the main tree and five clones:
- `fin`: final bytes `75c8aa17 04bc9ef6 d19e7a82 261c1ac7`;
- `s8`: S1-fix8 final;
- `d42`: a checkout of `d42fc517`;
- `aa5`: a checkout of `aa5cece0`;
- `cur`: a fresh clone of the main tree at `021e9405`, with the final bytes.
Every run used HOME=scratch, DISPLAY unset and `timeout`. The plants are in `rc/`, the logs in `logs/`, and the runner is
`battery.sh`/`plant.sh`, which restores every plant (0 "not restored"). The CLI gate ran in each case.

**Regression A: an honest near-miss that d42fc517 names now passes silently** (D19-B: "a stamp with its colon missing").
Each recipe is an ordinary markdown example. It is followed by a colon-less stamp in prose,
`**STAMP** `v1 claim=fixed tree=deadbee0 …``, and then by an ordinary ```` ```sh ```` fence. markdown-it 3.0.0 (commonmark)
renders every typo line as paragraph text (`render.py`, MEASURED).

| recipe (`rc/`) | preceding honest markdown | aa5 | d42 | S1-fix8 | **final** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `h_mdbq` | a ```` ```md ```` fence showing a blockquoted fence (`> ```text` / `> …` / `> ````) | ok | 1 problem | 1 problem | **ok** |
| `h_mdlist` | a ```` ```md ```` fence showing a list item with a 4-indented fence | ok | 1 | 1 | **ok** |
| `h_pydoc` | a ```` ```python ```` fence whose docstring holds an indented ```` ```sh ```` / ```` ``` ```` example | ok | 1 | 1 | **ok** |
| `h_indcode1` | an indented code block showing one ```` ```text ```` line ("a fence opens with a line like …") | ok | 1 | 1 | **ok** |
| `h_bqopen` | a blockquote holding only the start of a fence (`> ```sh` / `> make install`, then a blank line) | ok | 1 | 1 | **ok** |
| `j_mdbq_prose`, `j_mdlist_dash` | as above, with `The old stamp was **STAMP** `v1 …`` (the adv_slack line) / `**STAMP -** `v1 …`` | ok | 1 | 1 | **ok** |
| controls `h_ctl`, `h_ctl2` | the typo alone / after a plain fence | ok | 1 | 1 | 1 |

Every row was measured in stamped 1219 and in grandfathered 0057. The `h_mdbq`, `h_indcode1` and control rows were repeated
on `cur` (HEAD `021e9405`). The real corpus has no such shape: `census.tcl` found 0 col-0 prose lines that the stripped reading
puts inside a fence, over 1050 files. That is why the parser diff is 0; it is not a proof of safety. Spec §6 says a
```` ``` ```` line in an indented code block "is harmless, because the lines between are code either way". `h_indcode1`
refutes that sentence.

**Regression B: honest content that aa5cece0 accepts now goes red.** This is R1's own family: aa5 ok, d42 red, S1-fix8 ok. A
stamp-body *example* sits inside a real, closed, column-0 fence, *after* an inner container fence. markdown-it renders each
body line as fence content (MEASURED).

| recipe | aa5 | d42 | S1-fix8 | **final** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `h2_mdex`: ```` ```md ```` / `> ```text` / `> make install` / `> ```` / blank / `see `v1 claim=…`` / ```` ``` ```` | ok | 1 | ok | **1 problem** |
| `h2_mdlistex`: the same with a 4-indented list fence, then `2. Stamp it with `v1 claim=…`` | ok | 1 | ok | **1** |
| `h2_pyex`: a docstring fence example, then `    The body is `v1 claim=…`` | ok | 1 | ok | **1** |
| control `h2_ctl` (the body in a plain ```` ```md ```` fence) | ok | 1 | ok | ok |

These contradict the claim that "the fenced stamp examples are still ok". Every row was measured in 1219 and 0057, and
`h2_mdex`/`h2_pyex` again on `cur`.

**A measured remedy** (a prototype, clone `rem`, checker `6ac99c4c`, `body_ranges` in place of the one-line strip). It does
two things:
1. It blanks every line of each *raw* fence (opener through closer, or opener to EOF when unclosed) before the container-stripped scan.
2. It accepts a stripped fence's body range only while every line from its opener to its closer stays in the opener's container:
   `>` on every line when the opener is blockquoted, otherwise indentation at least the opener's on every non-blank line.
The union with the raw `inside` is what `stray_stamps` walks. Results:
- `rtest.tcl`: 15 of 15 answers right. `fin` gets 6 wrong and `s8` 4.
- The CLI battery: every `h_*` RED, every `h2_*` ok, and every `b_*`/`adv_*` identical to final. So b_bq and b_nested stay ok,
  and b_indcode, b_pre, b_unclosed_eof and b_inline stay RED.
- The suite: ALL PASS (100 checks).
- `stray_stamps` is identical to final on all 1050 committed files and on the 1056 files of the main tree's working directory
  (`sdiff.tcl`: 0 diffs).
- It also names both of spec §6's stated mis-pairing shapes: a blockquoted fence whose body line lost its `>`, and ```` ``` ````
  indented 4 under a paragraph. With it, that §6 limit can be deleted rather than stated.
- A suite row wants `h_mdbq`, `h_indcode1` and `h2_mdex`. Q22 has none of them.

**Reproduced (MEASURED, final bytes):**

### What it is: `d42fc517` plus items 1–5, and nothing else

It was built from S1-fix9's files, taking out what D21 withdraws (READ from the diff):
- **Removed: the in-fence stamp-body exemption and the container-stripped scan.** `fence_scan` no longer returns
  `inside`, so its dict is `d42fc517`'s shape again (`blocks opened`). `stray_stamps` is **byte-identical to
  `d42fc517`'s proc** (a `diff` of the two procs is empty). Its comment now records the D21 limit and the workaround.
- **Kept from S1-fix8/9:**
  - item 1: `fence_marks {quote assert path pat state}`, and an unmarked fence's info string is not read;
  - item 2: the typo'd key named as unknown, and the handoff of case/spacing near-misses to `stray_attrs`;
  - item 3: the CommonMark opener rule, plus `stray_attrs`' `info string holds a backtick` cause;
  - item 4: `t_scan_total` charged with scanning time only (`scan_charge`), plus `t_gate_total` 600 s, named by
    `gate_msg`;
  - item 5: `clip`, the fence's five named words plus a count, and `cap_lines` at 2000 characters.
- **Added, within item 2:** `Assert= absent` (the value came apart from its `=`) is now named once, not twice.
  S1-fix9's refuter MEASURED it at 2 problems on both `d42fc517` and S1-fix9. `fence_info` also leaves the next word
  to `stray_attrs` when a near-miss word is `key=` with an empty value and the next word has no `=`.

**Suite.**
- **Q19 and Q22 now hold the limit.** Q19 wants `{1 1 1 1 1 1 1 1 1}`: every body in a ```` ``` ````, `~~~` or
  four-backtick fence, stamped or grandfathered, is ONE problem, as is every prose shape. Q22 wants eleven 1s: the
  blockquoted and nested-list fences (grandfathered and stamped), an indented code block, `<pre>`, a blockquoted fence
  never closed and blockquoted prose, plus three new files built from S1-fix9's refuter's recipes:
  - 9261 = `h_mdbq`, a colon-less stamp in prose after a ```` ```md ```` example (S1-fix9's fail-open);
  - 9262 = `h_indcode1`;
  - 9263 = `h2_mdex`.
- **Q23** gains 9239 (`Assert= absent`, want `{1 0}`).
- **Q24's comment** says its first three parts now hold `d42fc517`'s behaviour. Its item-3 parts are the swallowed
  fence and the backtick cause.
- **Unchanged:** Q14, Q18, Q20 and Q21.

**Spec.**
- **§4** keeps S1-fix9's marking-key and "Which lines are fences" text. It now says the Slack line **swallowed a real
  fence**, not that it hid prose, and it adds `Assert= absent` to the near-miss list.
- **§5** says there is **no exception for a body inside a fence** and points to §6. It gives the real corpus as 17
  stamps at `32b6a9cd` (MEASURED: 17 body lines, all 17 of them `**STAMP:**` lines, in 17 files).
- **§6:**
  - a new bullet **"A stamp body inside a fence is named, as anywhere else"**, giving the D21 history and the workaround
    (show the example outside `doc/claude/issues/`, or break the body: `` `v1 …` `` without `claim=`, or no backtick
    before `v1`; the gate reads only `doc/claude/issues`, READ at `issue_stamp.tcl:333`);
  - the bullet "A stamp written in a fence is still read as one", kept without the exception's sentence;
  - "Which fences the block reader sees", with the container-stripped sub-bullet deleted;
  - a new bullet stating item 3's two consequences (below).
  - "A block that lost its marking key", the 600 s budget and the rest are unchanged.

### Deviations

1. **`Assert= absent` is named once (item 2, beyond S1-fix9).** The task names `ASSERT=`, `QUOTE=` and `assert =`;
   S1-fix9's refuter measured this spacing still named twice, on both `d42fc517` and S1-fix9. The change is five
   lines in `fence_info`. Q23's 9239, the self-test and sabotage A10 hold it.
2. **Q22 gained three files** (9261–9263: `h_mdbq`, `h_indcode1`, `h2_mdex`), which S1-fix9's refuter asked a row to
   want. They hold the limit against both exemptions (G8, G9).
3. **Item 3 has two ok → RED consequences against `d42fc517`**, `k_btpat_true` and `k_flip_true`. They are recorded
   as item-3 differences rather than regressions because markdown-it agrees with the final bytes (MEASURED) and both
   are named, never passed. They are stated in spec §6. **If the driver reads them as regressions, D21's rule keeps
   `d42fc517`.** This is the one judgement in this receipt that is not purely mechanical.
4. **The spec was edited once after the batteries.** Its md5 went `be5a86c2` → `93885228`: the corpus count 10 → 17
   and one reflowed paragraph. No code reads the spec: the only mention in either `.tcl` is a message string (READ).

## S1-fix10 — refutation (adversarial verifier, 2026-09-18)

**Verdict: REFUTED, on one regression family. There is no class-A finding.** It is MEASURED and fails open against
`d42fc517`. Item 3's opener rule (a ```` ``` ```` line whose info string holds a backtick opens no fence) changes how
fences pair up. A marked fence whose marking key is **misspelled** (`asert=`, `assertion=`, `asserts=`, `qoute=`,
`quotes=`) then stops being read, and stray_attrs cannot see it, because its test is `(quote|assert)[ \t]*=`. So in a
stamped file:
- `d42fc517` names the unknown key, rc 1;
- the final bytes say `ok (0 problems)`, rc 0.

The receipt does not disclose this. It says every item-3 consequence is fail-closed ("named, never passed"). Spec §5
still promises that "A misspelled marking key on a block (`asert=`, `qoute=`) is named", and D21 item 2 says "typo'd
marking keys are named".

Scratch: `/var/tmp/xschem_fixes/s1fix10_verify/`. It holds a `--no-hardlinks --mirror` of the main tree and these clones
of main HEAD `32b6a9cd`:
- `base1`–`base3` are untouched: `d42fc517`'s files, `6d0592ad 930eb435 d19e7a82 1d4f8efb`;
- `fin1`–`fin3` carry the final bytes, `ce91915b 9a81697d d19e7a82 93885228`.

Every run used HOME=scratch, DISPLAY unset and `timeout`. Every plant was restored, with 0 "not restored".

**The regression (MEASURED, CLI gate, stamped 1219; `logs/bat/`, `logs/compare.out`).** Each recipe is appended to 1219.
All the misspelled-key fences below make a FALSE claim: `SABOTAGE` has 8 hits under `src`, and the quote body is not in
`README`. "flip opener" means a line that item 3 turns into no fence.

| recipe (`rc/`) | shape | `d42fc517` | final |
|---|---|---|---|
| `z_flip_asert`, `_assertion`, `_asserts`, `_qoute`, `_quotes` | flip opener ```` ```sh `make` output ````, then its bare ```` ``` ````, then the misspelled fence | rc 1, `…has a key the grammar does not know` | **rc 0, ok** |
| `z_flip2_asert` | flip opener ```` ```bash # run `make check` first ```` | rc 1 | **rc 0, ok** |
| `z_flip3_qoute` | flip opener ```` ```console $ `pwd` ```` | rc 1 | **rc 0, ok** |
| `zr_real_asert`, `zr_real_qoute` (lanes `base2`/`fin2`) | a realistic file: ```` ```text `make check` output ```` / `FAIL: …` / ```` ``` ````, prose, then the misspelled fence | rc 1 | **rc 0, ok** |
| `z_bt_asert`, `z_bt_assertion`, `z_bt_qoute` | the misspelled key on the backtick-info line itself | rc 1 | **rc 0, ok** |

The controls hold:
- A canonical key in the same flip is still named on final, as `inside another fenced block`: `z_flip_false`,
  `z_flip_qrot`, `z_flip_multiword` and the `zr_real_ctl` control.
- `ASSERT=` is still named: `z_flip_ASSERT` and `z_bt_ASSERT`.
- Every recipe gives `ok` on both sides in grandfathered 0057.

Other measurements:
- **Cause (MEASURED, `scripts/gate3.tcl`, `gate` called directly, which skips the self-test).** The final checker with
  only the item-3 opener condition removed (`scripts/fin_S9e.tcl`) names all three probes again, byte-identical to
  `d42fc517`.
- **Scope (MEASURED, `scripts/fuzz3.tcl`, 20 000 random info strings per context):**
  - a fence standing alone gives **0** marked fences that `d42fc517` names and final passes;
  - after a flip opener it gives **2106**.
  - So items 1 and 2 are clean in isolation, and the hole is item 3's pairing only.
- **markdown-it 3.0.0 agrees with final's pairing** (MEASURED, `scripts/render.py`): the misspelled fence is content of
  the fence that opens at the bare ```` ``` ````. Final is CommonMark-correct about structure. What is lost is the name
  `d42fc517` gave an honest near-miss (D18-B).
- **`d42fc517` has the mirror-image hole:** after a Slack-style ```` ```make install``` fails ```` line, `z_r3_asert` and
  `z_r3_qoute` pass on `d42fc517` and are named on final.
- **The `z_bt_*` half was already visible.** S1-fix9's refuter's fuzz reported "new silent passes … apart from
  backtick-info lines". The flip-swallow half (`z_flip*`, `zr_real_*`) is new.
- **One possible fix, not built:** give stray_attrs the misspelled marking keys too, or name a marked word on any line
  that item 3 leaves unread.
